A derivation of braided C ∗ C^{*} -tensor categories from gapped ground states satisfying the approximate Haag duality
Yoshiko Ogata

Abstract
We derive braided C ∗ C^{*} -tensor categories from gapped ground states on two-dimensional quantum spin systems satisfying some additional condition which we call the approximate Haag duality.



1
Introduction

Recently, the classification problem of topologically ordered gapped
systems has attracted
a lot of attention.
It is frequently said that topological order is characterized by the

existence of anyons

and
long-range entanglement
.

It is well known that anyons show up in AQFT
[
BDMRS
]

[
BF
]

[
DHRI
]
[
FRS
]
[
K
]
[
BKLR
]

surprisingly naturally.
In Kitaev’s quantum double model, a famous model
of gapped ground state phase,
anyons also show up.
Applying the techniques of AQFT,
a detailed study of anyons
in Kitaev’s quantum double models in the operator algebraic setting
was carried out by P. Naaijkens and his coauthors

[
N1
]
[
N2
]
[
FN
]
[
CNN1
]
.
Furthermore, in
[
CNN1
]
Cha-Naaijkens-Nachtergaele
derived a braided
C^{*}
-tensor category in a general setting of
semi-group of almost localized endomorphisms.

On the other hand, in
[
NO
]
, we proved that superselection sectors are topological invariant.
In particular,
the existence of a non-trivial
superselection sector implies the long-range entanglement of the system.
There is folklore saying that the existence of
anyons implies long-range entanglement of the state.
This motivates us to derive braided
C^{*}
-tensor categories out of
superselection sectors, where, unlike endomorphisms, the multiplication rule is not apriori given.
In this paper, we derive braided
C^{*}
-tensor categories out of
non-trivial
sector theory provided an additional condition,
the approximate Haag duality.
In AQFT, a braided
C^{*}
-tensor category
is usually derived
under Haag duality
[
BF
]

[
DHRI
]
[
FRS
]
[
K
]
[
BKLR
]
, except for
[
BDMRS
]
.
Haag duality itself looks too strong to be required in gapped ground phases of
quantum spin systems, although in very nice models
like Kitaev quantum double models, it holds
[
N1
]
[
N2
]
[
FN
]
.
In particular, it does not look plausible that this property is stable under
quasi-local automorphisms, which are fundamental operations
in our classification problem
[
BMNS
]
[
NSY
]
.
The approximate Haag duality is a relaxation of Haag duality.
The good point about this approximate version is that it is stable under quasi-local automorphisms.
Our derivation of the braided
C^{*}
-tensor category basically
goes parallel to the recipe from AQFT. However, the “approximateness” of the Haag duality
and the other differences of our setting from AQFT require additional arguments.

1.1
Two-dimensional quantum spin systems

We start by summarizing standard setup of
2
-dimensional quantum spin systems on the two dimensional lattice
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}

[
BR1
,
BR2
]
.
Throughout this paper, we fix some
2\leq d\in\mathbb{N}
.
We denote the algebra of
d\times d
matrices by
\mathop{\mathrm{M}}\nolimits_{d}
.
For each
z\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, let
{\mathcal{A}}_{\{z\}}
be an isomorphic copy of
\mathop{\mathrm{M}}\nolimits_{d}
, and for any finite subset
\Lambda\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, we set
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}=\bigotimes_{z\in\Lambda}{\mathcal{A}}_{\{z\}}
.
For finite
\Lambda
, the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
can be regarded as the set of all bounded operators acting on
the Hilbert space
\bigotimes_{z\in\Lambda}{{\mathbb{C}}}^{d}
.
We use this identification freely.
If
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}
, the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}}
is naturally embedded in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}
by tensoring its elements with the identity.
For an infinite subset
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,

{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}

is given as the inductive limit of the algebras
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
with
\Lambda
, finite subsets of
\Gamma
.
We call
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
the quantum spin system on
\Gamma
.
For a subset
\Gamma_{1}
of
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,
the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma_{1}}
can be regarded as a subalgebra of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}
.
For
\Gamma\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, with a bit abuse of notation, we write
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma}

to denote
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
Also,
\Gamma^{c}
denotes the complement of
\Gamma
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.
We regularly use the notations and facts about cones
collected in Appendix
A
.

1.2
Approximate Haag duality

A representation
({\mathcal{H}},\pi_{0})
is said to satisfy
the Haag duality if
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}=\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}
.
The corresponding (original) condition is broadly assumed in AQFT.
The problem for us about introducing this condition in quantum spin systems
is that it does not look to be plausible that this condition is stable under
quasi-local automorphisms,
the fundamental operation in the analysis of gappd ground state phases.
For this reason, we introduce a weaker version of Haag duality.

Definition 1.1
.

[Approximate Haag duality]
Let
({\mathcal{H}},\pi_{0})
be an irreducible representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
We say that
({\mathcal{H}},\pi_{0})
satisfies the approximate Haag duality if
the following condition holds.:
For any
\varphi\in(0,2\pi)
and

\varepsilon>0
with

\varphi+4\varepsilon<2\pi
,
there is some
R_{\varphi,\varepsilon}>0
and decreasing
functions
f_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\delta}(t)
,
\delta>0

on
{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}

with
\lim_{t\to\infty}f_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\delta}(t)=0

such that

(i)

for any cone
\Lambda
with
|\arg\Lambda|=\varphi
, there is a unitary

U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}})

satisfying

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda-R_{\varphi,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right),

(1.1)

and

(ii)

for any
\delta>0
and
t\geq 0
, there is a unitary

\tilde{U}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon,\delta,t}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\varepsilon+\delta}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}-\tilde{U}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon,\delta,t}\right\|\leq f_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\delta}(t).

(1.2)

The good point about this weaker version is that we know it is stable under quasi-local automorphisms.
Quasi-local automorphisms are automorphisms given by time-dependent interactions
satisfying suitable locality conditions
[
NSY
]
.
We will not repeat the definition here. The only property of
quasi-local automorphisms we need is the following factorization property.

Definition 1.2
.

Let
\alpha
be an automorphism of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
We say that
\alpha
is approximately-factorizable
if the following condition holds.

(i)

For any cone
\Lambda
and
\delta>0
,
there are automorphisms
\beta_{\Lambda},\tilde{\beta}_{\Lambda}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)
,

\beta_{\Lambda^{c}},\tilde{\beta}_{\Lambda^{c}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)

and
\Xi_{\Lambda,\delta},\tilde{\Xi}_{\Lambda,\delta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\delta}\cap(\Lambda^{c})_{\delta}}\right)

and unitaries
v_{\Lambda\delta},\tilde{v}_{\Lambda\delta}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\alpha=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\Lambda\delta}\right)\circ\Xi_{\Lambda,\delta}\circ\left(\beta_{\Lambda}\otimes\beta_{\Lambda^{c}}\right),\\
&\alpha^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda\delta}\right)\circ\tilde{\Xi}_{\Lambda,\delta}\circ\left(\tilde{\beta}_{\Lambda}\otimes\tilde{\beta}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right).\end{split}

(1.3)

(ii)

For each
\delta,\delta^{\prime}>0,\varphi\in(0,2\pi)
, there exists a decreasing
function
g_{\varphi,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}(t)
on
{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}

with
\lim_{t\to\infty}g_{\varphi,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}(t)=0
.
For any cone
\Lambda
with
\varphi=|\arg\Lambda|
, there are unitaries

v_{\Lambda,\delta,\delta^{\prime},t}^{\prime},\tilde{v}_{\Lambda,\delta,\delta^{\prime},t}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\delta+\delta^{\prime}}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}

satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|v_{\Lambda,\delta}-v_{\Lambda,\delta,\delta^{\prime},t}^{\prime}\right\|,\left\|\tilde{v}_{\Lambda,\delta}-\tilde{v}_{\Lambda,\delta,\delta^{\prime},t}^{\prime}\right\|\leq g_{\varphi,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}(t),

(1.4)

for unitaries
v_{\Lambda\delta},\tilde{v}_{\Lambda\delta}
in (i).

The approximate Haag duality is stable under the approximately-factorizable automorphisms.

Proposition 1.3
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}},\pi_{0})
be an irreducible representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

satisfying the approximate Haag duality.
Then for any approximately-factorizable automorphism
\alpha
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,

({\mathcal{H}},\pi_{0}\circ\alpha)
also satisfies the approximate Haag duality.

We use the notation in Definition
1.1
and Definition
1.2
.
For each

\varphi\in(0,2\pi)
and

\varepsilon>0
with

\varphi+4\varepsilon<2\pi
, we fix
\delta>0

so that

\displaystyle 16\delta<\varepsilon,\quad\varphi+16\delta<2\pi.

(1.5)

and set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&R_{\varphi,\varepsilon}^{(1)}:=R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta},\\
&f_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\delta_{0}}^{(1)}(t):=\left\{\begin{gathered}g_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t-R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}}{2}\right)+2g_{\varphi+6\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t-R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}}{2}\right)+f_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t-R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}}{2}\right)\\
+g_{\varphi+4\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t-R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}}{2}\right)+2g_{\varphi+8\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t-R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}}{2}\right),\quad\text{if}\quad t\geq R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta},\\
2+6\left\|g\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|f\right\|_{\infty},\quad\text{if}\quad 0\leq t\leq R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}.\end{gathered}\right.\end{split}

(1.6)

for
\delta_{0}>0
.

We show (i), (ii) of Definition
1.1
for this

f_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\delta_{0}}^{(1)}(t)
,
R_{\varphi,\varepsilon}^{(1)}
.
Let
\Lambda
be any cone with
|\arg\Lambda|=\varphi
. We have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{\delta}\right)^{c}}\right)=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\alpha^{-1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{\delta}\right)^{c}}\right)=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right)\circ\tilde{\Xi}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\circ\left(\tilde{\beta}_{\Lambda_{\delta}}\otimes\tilde{\beta}_{(\Lambda_{\delta})^{c}}\right)\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{\delta}\right)^{c}}\right)\\
\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{0}\left(\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right)\right)\right)\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})\right).\end{split}

(1.7)

We use the notation

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}:=\Lambda-R_{|\arg\Lambda|+2\delta,\delta}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}.

(1.8)

Taking the commutant of (
1.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right)\right)\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}\right)\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{\delta}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\\
&\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\tilde{\Lambda}\right)_{2\delta}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\alpha\alpha^{-1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\tilde{\Lambda}\right)_{2\delta}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\pi_{0}\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2\delta},\delta}\right)\right)\left(\left(\pi_{0}\alpha\circ\tilde{\Xi}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2\delta},\delta}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2\delta}}\right)\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\\
&\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\pi_{0}\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2\delta},\delta}\right)\right)\left(\pi_{0}\alpha\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3\delta}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\end{split}

(1.9)

Hence we obtain

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\circ\alpha({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right)^{*}U_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\pi_{0}\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2\delta},\delta}\right)\right)\left(\pi_{0}\alpha\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{3\delta}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right).

(1.10)

Note that

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{3\delta}=\left(\Lambda-R_{|\arg\Lambda|+2\delta,\delta}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)_{3\delta}\subset\left(\Lambda-R_{\varphi,\varepsilon}^{(1)}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)_{\varepsilon}.

(1.11)

Hence setting

\displaystyle U_{\Lambda\epsilon}^{(1)}:=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right)^{*}U_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\pi_{0}\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2\delta},\delta}\right),

(1.12)

we obtain

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\circ\alpha({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda\epsilon}^{(1)}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\alpha\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda-R_{\varphi,\varepsilon}^{(1)}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right),

(1.13)

proving (i).
For any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\circ\Xi_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda},\delta}\circ\beta_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\left(\tilde{v}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\right\|\\
&\leq\left\|\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right)-\alpha\left(\tilde{v}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\right\|\\
&+2\left\|v^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}-v_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda},\delta}\right\|\\
&\leq g_{|\arg\Lambda|+2\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)+2g_{|\arg\Lambda|+6\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)=g_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)+2g_{\varphi+6\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right).\end{split}

(1.14)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{2\delta},\delta}\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v^{\prime}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{4\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\circ\Xi_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{4\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}},\delta}\circ\beta_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{4\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}}}\left(\tilde{v}^{\prime}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{2\delta},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\right\|\\
&\leq g_{\varphi+4\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)+2g_{\varphi+8\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right).\end{split}

(1.15)

For each
t\geq 0
, set

\displaystyle\begin{split}{U_{t}}:&=\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\circ\Xi_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda},\delta}\circ\beta_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\left(\tilde{v^{\prime}}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\right)^{*}\cdot\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\\
&\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v^{\prime}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{4\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\circ\Xi_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{4\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}},\delta}\circ\beta_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{4\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}}}\left(\tilde{v}^{\prime}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{2\delta},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\right)\\
&\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{5\delta}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cdot\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{6\delta}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{6\delta}-\left(R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}+t\right)}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}.\end{split}

(1.16)

Because

\displaystyle{\Lambda_{6\delta}-\left(R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}+t\right)}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda_{\varepsilon+\delta_{0}}-\left(t+R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}\right)\bm{e}_{\Lambda}=\Lambda_{\varepsilon+\delta_{0}}-\left(t+R_{\varphi,\varepsilon}^{(1)}\right)\bm{e}_{\Lambda},

(1.17)

we have

\displaystyle{U_{t}}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\varepsilon+\delta_{0}}-\left(t+R_{\varphi,\varepsilon}^{(1)}\right)\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

(1.18)

for any
t\geq 0
and
\delta_{0}>0
.
For this
U_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|U_{t}-U_{\Lambda\epsilon}^{(1)}\right\|\\
&\leq g_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)+2g_{\varphi+6\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)+f_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)+g_{\varphi+4\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)+2g_{\varphi+8\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)=f_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\delta_{0}}^{(1)}\left(t+R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}\right).\end{split}

(1.19)

Hence setting

\displaystyle{\tilde{U}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon,\delta_{0},t}}^{(1)}:=\left\{\begin{gathered}U_{t-R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}},\quad t\geq R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta},\\
\mathbb{I},\quad t<R_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta}\end{gathered}\right.,

we obtain

\displaystyle{\tilde{U}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon,\delta_{0},t}}^{(1)}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\varepsilon+\delta_{0}}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime},

(1.22)

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\tilde{U}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon,\delta_{0},t}}^{(1)}-U_{\Lambda\epsilon}^{(1)}\right\|\leq f_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\delta_{0}}^{(1)}\left(t\right).

(1.23)

This proves (ii).

\square

From the proof of the proposition, we obtain the following Lemma which we will use later.

Lemma 1.4
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}},\pi_{0})
be a representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

satisfying the approximate Haag duality and
\alpha

an approximately-factorizable automorphism
\alpha
on
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
With the notations in Definition
1.1
and Definition
1.2
,
for any cone
\Lambda
,
\delta>0
,
t\geq R_{|\arg\Lambda|+2\delta,\delta}
with
|\arg\Lambda|+16\delta<2\pi
,
there is a unitary
\tilde{w}_{\Lambda\delta t}
on
{\mathcal{H}}

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{w}_{\Lambda\delta t}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}\right)\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{5\delta}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

(1.24)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\tilde{w}_{\Lambda,\delta,t}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq g_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)+2g_{\varphi+6\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right)+f_{\varphi+2\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{t}{2}\right),

(1.25)

with
\varphi=|\Lambda|
.

With the notation in the proof of Proposition
1.3
,
set

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{w}_{\Lambda,\delta,t}:=&\pi_{0}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\circ\Xi_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda},\delta}\circ\beta_{\Lambda_{3\delta}-\frac{t}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\left(\tilde{v}^{\prime}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}\right)\right)^{*}\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta,\delta,\frac{t}{2}}U_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}^{*}\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda_{\delta},\delta}\right).\end{split}

(1.26)

\square

1.3
Main result

Let us recall the definition of the superselection criterion.
For representations
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
of a
C^{*}
-algebra, we write
\pi_{1}\simeq_{u.e.}\pi_{2}

if they are unitarily equivalent.

Definition 1.5
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}},\pi_{0})
be an irreducible representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
We say a representation
\pi
of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
on
{\mathcal{H}}

satisfies the superselection criterion for
\pi_{0}
if

\pi|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}\simeq_{u.e.}\pi_{0}|_{{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}},

for any cone
\Lambda
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.
We say that
\pi_{0}
has a trivial sector theory if
any representation satisfying the superselection criterion for
\pi_{0}

is quasi-equivalent to
\pi_{0}
.
Otherwise, we say
\pi_{0}
has a non-trivial sector theory.

In this paper, we show the following theorem.
For the more precise statement, see Theorem
5.2
and Theorem
6.1
.

Theorem
.

Let
\omega_{\Phi}
be a pure ground state of a uniformly bounded finite range interaction
\Phi

on
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

satisfying the gap condition. Suppose that its
GNS representation satisfies
the approximate Haag duality and has a non-trivial super selection sector.
Then its superselection sectors
form a braided
C^{*}
-tensor category.
If two of such states
\omega_{\Phi_{1}},\omega_{\Phi_{2}}

are connected via an approximately factorizable automorphism
\alpha

as
\omega_{\Phi_{2}}=\omega_{\Phi_{1}}\circ\alpha
,
then corresponding braided
C^{*}
-tensor categories
are monoidal equivalent.

In section
2
, following
[
BF
]
,
we extend our superselection sectors to a larger
C^{*}
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
As in
[
BF
]
, this allows us to define the composition
of superselection sectors (section
3
), which gives the tensor
of the object in our braided tensor category.
In section
4
, we introduce tensor for the morphisms
(i.e., intertwiners) and further obtain braiding operators, following the argument in
[
DHRI
]

.
Everything from section
2
to section
4

is parallel to that of
[
BF
]
and
[
DHRI
]
, but the fact that our Haag duality
is only an approximate one requires some additional estimate.
In section
5
, we prove that
our superselection sectors form a braided
C^{*}
-tensor category.
Here, we need the gap condition to show the existence of
direct sums and subobjects.
This part requires arguments that are different from those in AQFT.
We then show the monoidal equivalence between the braided
C^{*}
-categories
obtained from states related by an approximate-factorizable automorphism
in section
6
.

2
Superselection sectors and their extensions

Throughout this section we consider the following setting.

Setting 2.1
.

Let
({\mathcal{H}},\pi_{0})
be an irreducible representation of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
We denote by
{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
the set of all representations of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
on
{\mathcal{H}}

satisfying the superselection criterion for
\pi_{0}
.
For
\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
and
R\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
,
we say that
R
is an intertwiner from
\rho
to
\sigma

if

\displaystyle R\rho(A)=\sigma(A)R,\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}.

(2.1)

We denote by
(\rho,\sigma)
the set of all intertwiners from
\rho
to
\sigma
.
For each
\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
and a cone

\Lambda
, we denote by
{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda}

the set of all unitaries
V_{\rho,\Lambda}

satisfying

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho,\Lambda}\right)\circ\rho|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}=\pi_{0}|_{{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}}.

(2.2)

(By
\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
,
{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda}
is not empty.)

Lemma 2.2
.

Consider Setting
2.1
.
Let
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
be cones,
\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
, and

V_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}
,
V_{\sigma,\Lambda_{2}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{2}}
.
Let
R\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
be an intertwiner from
\rho
to
\sigma
, i.e.,

\sigma(A)R=R\rho(A)
for
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
Then
the operator

V_{\sigma,\Lambda_{2}}RV_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{*}

belongs to
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime}
.

For any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2})^{c}}
,
we have

\displaystyle V_{\sigma,\Lambda_{2}}RV_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{*}\pi_{0}(A)=V_{\sigma,\Lambda_{2}}R\rho(A)V_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{*}=V_{\sigma,\Lambda_{2}}\sigma(A)RV_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{*}=\pi_{0}(A)V_{\rho,\Lambda_{2}}RV_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{*},

(2.3)

by the definition of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{1}},{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{2}}
and the fact
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{(\Lambda_{1}\cup\Lambda_{2})^{c}}
.

\square

We introduce a
C^{*}
-subalgebra of
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

Definition 2.3
.

Consider Setting
2.1
. For each cone
\Lambda
, we set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda):=\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime}.

(2.4)

For each
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
we set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}:=\overline{\cup_{\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}}\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)}.

(2.5)

Here
\overline{\cdot}
denotes the norm closure.
(See Appendix
A
(
A.7
) for the definition of
{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.)

Note that
\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{1})\subset\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2})

if
\Lambda_{1}\subset\Lambda_{2}
.
Because
{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
is an upward filtering set,

{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
is a
C^{*}
-algebra.
For each
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})\subset\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime}=\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}.

(2.6)

By Lemma
A.3
, this implies that

\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}})
is a
C^{*}
-subalgebra of
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.

Lemma 2.4
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
The unitary
U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}
in Definition
1.1

belongs to
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
The
*
-algebra
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}:=\cup_{\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}}\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}

is norm-dense in
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.

By the definition (
2.5
), it suffices to show that
for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,
\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)
is in the norm colosure
\overline{{\mathcal{B}}_{0}}

of
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
.
For any cone
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, choose

\varepsilon>0
so that

\Lambda_{4\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
For each
t\geq 0
and
\delta>0
,

\tilde{U}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon,\delta,t}
in Definition
1.1
belongs to

\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{|\arg\Lambda|+\delta}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,
and we have
\lim_{t\to\infty}\left\|U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}-\tilde{U}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon,\delta,t}\right\|=0
.
Hence we have

U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\in\overline{{\mathcal{B}}_{0}}
.
Furthermore
we have
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda-R_{|\arg\Lambda|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{0}

because
\left(\Lambda-R_{|\arg\Lambda|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
Hence

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon})\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda-R_{|\arg\Lambda|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset\overline{{\mathcal{B}}_{0}}.

(2.7)

\square

We also note the following from the approximate Haag duality.

Lemma 2.5
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
For any cone
\Lambda
, a unitary
u_{\Lambda}\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)
,

\varepsilon,\delta>0
with
|\arg\Lambda|+4\varepsilon<2\pi

and
t\geq R_{|\arg\Lambda|,\varepsilon}
,
there is some unitary
\tilde{u}_{\Lambda}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\varepsilon+\delta}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

such that
\left\|u_{\Lambda}-\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\Lambda}}\right\|\leq 2f_{|\arg\Lambda|,\varepsilon,\delta}(t)
.

Set

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\Lambda}}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{U}_{\Lambda\varepsilon\delta t}U_{\Lambda\varepsilon}^{*}\right)\left(u_{\Lambda}\right).

(2.8)

\square

Definition 2.6
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
We say that a set of cones
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}

is distal from a set of cones
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}

if there are cones
\tilde{\Lambda}_{1},\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}
and
\varepsilon>0

such that

\displaystyle\cup_{\Lambda_{1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}\Lambda_{1}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{1},\quad\cup_{\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}\Lambda_{2}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}

(2.9)

and

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}-R_{|\arg\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}}\right)_{\varepsilon}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}^{c}.

(2.10)

We also say
\Lambda_{1}
is distal from
\Lambda_{2}
if

\{\Lambda_{1}\}
is distal from
\{\Lambda_{2}\}
.
Note that if
\Lambda_{1}
is distal from
\Lambda_{2}
then
\Lambda_{1}\cap\Lambda_{2}=\emptyset
.

Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
.
We say that a set of cones
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

is distal from a set of cones
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

with the forbidden direction
{(\theta,\varphi)}

if
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}

is distal from
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
and the above
cones
\tilde{\Lambda}_{1},\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}
and
\varepsilon>0
can be taken so that

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\right)_{\varepsilon},\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}\right)_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)},\quad\arg\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\right)_{\varepsilon}\cap\arg\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}\right)_{\varepsilon}=\emptyset.

(2.11)

We write

{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\perp_{(\theta,\varphi)}{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
if

{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

is distal from a set of cones
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

with the forbidden direction
{(\theta,\varphi)}
, and

{\mathcal{S}}_{2}

is distal from a set of cones
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}

with the forbidden direction
{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
Furthermore, for such
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\perp_{(\theta,\varphi)}{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
,
we denote by
{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{S}}_{2})
the set of

(\varepsilon,\tilde{\Lambda}_{1},\tilde{\Lambda}_{2})

satisfying (
2.9
), (
2.10
) (
2.11
),
and

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}-R_{|\arg\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}}\right)_{\varepsilon}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}^{c}.

(2.12)

Lemma 2.7
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
be finite sets of
cones such that
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\perp_{(\theta,\varphi)}{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
.
Let
(\varepsilon,\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}

\tilde{\Lambda}_{2})\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{S}}_{2})
.
Then for any
t\geq 0
,
there are
t_{\Lambda_{1}}\geq 0
,

t_{\Lambda_{2}}\geq 0
for each
\Lambda_{1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
,
\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
,
such that

\displaystyle\cup_{\Lambda_{1}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}\left(\Lambda_{1}+t_{\Lambda_{1}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}},\quad\cup_{\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{2}}\left(\Lambda_{1}+t_{\Lambda_{2}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}+t\bm{e}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}}.

(2.13)

This is immediate from Lemma
A.2
.

\square

Lemma 2.8
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\Lambda_{1}
,
\Lambda_{2}
be cones such that

\Lambda_{1}
is distal from
\Lambda_{2}
.
Let
X^{t_{1}}\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}})

and
X^{t_{2}}\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}})
be
operators
given for each
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
.
Suppose that
\sup_{t_{i}\geq 0}\left\|X^{t_{i}}\right\|<\infty
,
i=1,2
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left\|X^{t_{1}}X^{t_{2}}-X^{t_{2}}X^{t_{1}}\right\|=0.

(2.14)

By definition, there are cones

\tilde{\Lambda}_{1},\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}
and
\varepsilon>0

such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\subset\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}-R_{|\arg\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}}\right)_{\varepsilon}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}^{c},\quad\Lambda_{2}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}.

(2.15)

Note that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\subset\Lambda_{1}+\frac{t_{1}}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\subset\Lambda_{1}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\subset\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}-R_{|\arg\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}}\right)_{\varepsilon}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}^{c}\subset\Lambda_{2}^{c}\subset\left(\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{c},

(2.16)

for
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
.
From (
1.1
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}})\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{1}+\left(t_{1}-R_{|\arg\Lambda_{1}|,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\right)\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)_{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\\
&\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right),\end{split}

(2.17)

for
t_{1}\geq R_{|\arg\Lambda_{1}|,\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}
.
Therefore,
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{*}\right)\left(X^{t_{1}}\right)\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

and
X^{t_{2}}\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}})

commute.
Furthermore,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}X^{t_{2}}-X^{t_{2}}U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\right\|\\
&\leq 2\left\|U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}-\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{t_{1}}{2}}\right\|\left\|X^{t_{2}}\right\|\leq 2f_{|\arg\Lambda_{1}|,\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\left(\frac{t_{1}}{2}\right)\left\|X^{t_{2}}\right\|\end{split}

(2.18)

because of (
2.16
)
and

\displaystyle U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2},\frac{t_{1}}{2}}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}-\frac{t_{1}}{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}.

(2.19)

An analogous estimate holds for
\left(X^{t_{2}}\right)^{*}
.
Hence we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left\|X^{t_{1}}X^{t_{2}}-X^{t_{2}}X^{t_{1}}\right\|=\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left\|U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{*}\left(X^{t_{1}}X^{t_{2}}-X^{t_{2}}X^{t_{1}}\right)U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\right\|\\
&=\lim_{t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty}\left\|\left[\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}^{*}\right)\left(X^{t_{1}}\right),X^{t_{2}}\right]\right\|=0.\end{split}

(2.20)

\square

Lemma 2.9
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
.
Let
{\mathbb{A}}_{1},{\mathbb{A}}_{2}\subset{\mathbb{T}}
be closed intervals such that

\left({\mathbb{A}}_{1}\cup{\mathbb{A}}_{2}\right)\cap{\mathbb{A}}_{[\theta-\varphi,\theta+\varphi]}=\emptyset

and
{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\cap{\mathbb{A}}_{2}=\emptyset
.
Then for any
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{\prime},\Lambda_{2},\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

with

\arg\Lambda_{i},\arg\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{i},\quad i=1,2,

there are
L_{i},L_{i}^{\prime}\geq 0
,
i=1,2

such that

\displaystyle\{\Lambda_{1}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}+L_{1}^{\prime}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\}\perp_{(\theta,\varphi)}\{\Lambda_{2}+L_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}},\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}+L_{2}^{\prime}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\}.

(2.21)

We may write
{\mathbb{A}}_{1}={\mathbb{A}}_{I_{1}}
,
{\mathbb{A}}_{2}={\mathbb{A}}_{I_{2}}
with closed
intervals
I_{i}=\theta_{i}+[-\varphi_{i},\varphi_{i}]
,
i=1,2
in
{\mathbb{R}}
. Fix some
\varepsilon>0
such that

{\mathbb{A}}_{\left(\theta_{1}+[-\varphi_{1}+\varepsilon,\varphi_{1}+\varepsilon]\right)}\cap{\mathbb{A}}_{\left(\theta_{2}+[-\varphi_{2}-\varepsilon,\varphi_{2}+\varepsilon]\right)}=\emptyset
and

{\mathbb{A}}_{\left(\theta_{i}+[-\varphi_{i}-\varepsilon,\varphi_{i}+\varepsilon]\right)}\cap{\mathbb{A}}_{{[\theta-\varphi,\theta+\varphi]}}=\emptyset
,
i=1,2
.
Set

\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}:=\Lambda_{\bm{0},\theta_{i},\varphi_{i}}+(R_{2\varphi_{i},\varepsilon}+1)\bm{e}_{\theta_{i}}
,

i=1,2
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}-R_{|\arg\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}}\right)_{\varepsilon}=\Lambda_{\bm{0},\theta_{1},\varphi_{1}+\varepsilon}+\bm{e}_{\theta_{1}}\subset\left(\Lambda_{\bm{0},\theta_{2},\varphi_{2}}\right)^{c}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}^{c},

(2.22)

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}-R_{|\arg\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}}\right)_{\varepsilon}=\Lambda_{\bm{0},\theta_{2},\varphi_{2}+\varepsilon}+\bm{e}_{\theta_{2}}\subset\left(\Lambda_{\bm{0},\theta_{1},\varphi_{1}}\right)^{c}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}^{c},

(2.23)

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}\right)_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)},\quad i=1,2,

(2.24)

\displaystyle\arg\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\right)_{\varepsilon}\cap\arg\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}\right)_{\varepsilon}=\emptyset.

(2.25)

By Lemma
A.2
, there are
L_{i},L_{i}^{\prime}\geq 0
,
i=1,2

such that

\displaystyle\{\Lambda_{i}+L_{i}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{i}},\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}+L_{i}^{\prime}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{i}}\}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}.

(2.26)

This proves the claim.

\square

In order to introduce the extension of
\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}

to
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, we prepare the following sets.

Definition 2.10
.

Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
, and
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
Then we have
\Lambda=\Lambda_{\bm{p},\bar{\theta},\bar{\varphi}}

with some
\bm{p}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,
\bar{\theta}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\bar{\varphi}\in(0,\pi)

satisfying

\displaystyle\theta+\varphi<\bar{\theta}-\bar{\varphi}<\bar{\theta}+\bar{\varphi}<\theta-\varphi+2\pi.

(2.27)

We denote by
\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}

the set of all cones
K_{\Lambda}

distal from
\Lambda
with the forbidden direction
{(\theta,\varphi)}
,
satisfying
\arg K_{\Lambda}\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{(\theta+\varphi,\bar{\theta}-\bar{\varphi})}
.

Clearly, we have
\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.

Lemma 2.11
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
, and
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
Let
K_{\Lambda},\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}\in\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}
.
Then there are
L_{1},\tilde{L}_{1}\geq 0

such that

\{K_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{K_{\Lambda}},\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}+\tilde{L}_{1}\bm{e}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}\}
is distal from
\{\Lambda\}
with the forbidden direction

(\theta,\varphi)
.

Writing
\Lambda=\Lambda_{\bm{p},\bar{\theta},\bar{\varphi}}

with
\bm{p}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
and
\bar{\theta},\bar{\varphi}
satisfying (
2.27
),
there are
\beta,\gamma\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\varepsilon>0
such that

\theta+\varphi<\beta-\gamma-\varepsilon<\beta+\gamma+\varepsilon<\bar{\theta}-\bar{\varphi}

and

\arg K_{\Lambda},\arg\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}\subset{\mathbb{A}}_{[\beta-\gamma,\beta+\gamma]},\quad\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}.

Set

\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}:=\Lambda_{\bm{p},\beta,\gamma}+(R_{2\gamma,\varepsilon}+1)\bm{e}_{\beta}
,
and
\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}:=\Lambda
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}-R_{|\arg\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}}\right)_{\varepsilon}=\Lambda_{\bm{p},\beta,\gamma+\varepsilon}+\bm{e}_{\beta}\subset\left(\Lambda_{\bm{p},\bar{\theta},\bar{\varphi}}\right)^{c}=\Lambda^{c}=\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}^{c},

(2.28)

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{i}\right)_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)},\quad i=1,2

(2.29)

\displaystyle\arg\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\right)_{\varepsilon}\cap\arg\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}\right)_{\varepsilon}=\emptyset.

(2.30)

By Lemma
A.2
, there are
L_{1},\tilde{L}_{1}\geq 0
,
such that

\displaystyle\{K_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{K_{\Lambda}},\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}+\tilde{L}_{1}\bm{e}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}\}\subset\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}.

(2.31)

This proves the claim.

\square

Lemma 2.12
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,

\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
and
\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}
.
For any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, the followings hold.

(i)

For any
K_{\Lambda}\in\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}
and
V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\left(\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)\right)\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)},

(2.32)

and

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\right)\circ\rho(A),\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}.

(2.33)

In particular, there is some
C_{\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

such that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\left(\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)\right)\subset\mathfrak{A}(C_{\Lambda})
for each
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.

(ii)

For any
K_{\Lambda},\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}\in\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}

and

V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}
,

V_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\left.\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\right|_{\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}}=\left.\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\right|_{\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}}.

(2.34)

(i)
Because
{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
is an upward filtering set, for
\Lambda,\Lambda_{0},K_{\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

there is a
C_{\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

such that
\Lambda,\Lambda_{0},K_{\Lambda}\subset C_{\Lambda}
.
From Lemma
2.2
, we have

\displaystyle\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{0}\cup K_{\Lambda}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\Lambda}^{c}}\right)^{\prime}=\mathfrak{A}(C_{\Lambda})\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}.

(2.35)

Hence we conclude (
2.32
) and
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\left(\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)\right)\subset\mathfrak{A}(C_{\Lambda})
.
By the definition, we have
\Lambda\subset K_{\Lambda}^{c}
for

K_{\Lambda}\in\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}
.
Therefore, by the definition of
{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}
(Setting
2.1
),
for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{K_{\Lambda}^{c}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\right)\circ\rho(A),

(2.36)

proving (
2.33
).

(ii)

For any
K_{\Lambda},\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}\in\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}
, there are

L_{1},\tilde{L}_{1}\geq 0

such that

\{K_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{K_{\Lambda}},\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}+\tilde{L}_{1}\bm{e}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}\}
is distal from
\{\Lambda\}
with the forbidden direction

(\theta,\varphi)
, by Lemma
2.11
.
Namely, there is a cone
\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

including
K_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{K_{\Lambda}},\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}+\tilde{L}_{1}\bm{e}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}
,
such that
\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\cap\Lambda=\emptyset
.
Note that
\Lambda\subset K_{\Lambda}^{c}\cap\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\right)^{c}\cap\left(\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}\right)^{c}
.
By Lemma
2.2
, all of

\displaystyle\begin{split}&V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{K_{\Lambda}}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{K_{\Lambda}^{c}}\right)^{\prime}=\mathfrak{A}(K_{\Lambda}),\\
&V_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{K_{\Lambda}}}^{*}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}\right)\cup\left(K_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{K_{\Lambda}}\right)\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime}=\mathfrak{A}(\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}),\\
&V_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}}^{*}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}^{c}}\right)^{\prime}=\mathfrak{A}(\tilde{K}_{\Lambda})\end{split}

(2.37)

commute with
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}
.
As a result,

\displaystyle V_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}=V_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}}^{*}V_{\rho,\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{\tilde{K}_{\Lambda}}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{K_{\Lambda}}}^{*}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}+L_{1}\bm{e}_{K_{\Lambda}}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}

(2.38)

commutes with
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
, proving the claim.

\square

Definition 2.13
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,

\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
,
\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}
, and

\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
From Lemma
2.12

we may define a
\sigma
w-continuous
*
-homomorphism

\displaystyle T_{\rho,\Lambda}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}:\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

(2.39)

by

\displaystyle T_{\rho,\Lambda}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}:=\left.\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\right|_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}},

(2.40)

independent of choice of
K_{\Lambda}\in\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}
and
V_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,K_{\Lambda}}
.

Lemma 2.14
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,

\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
,
\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}
, and

\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
Then there exists a
*
-homomorphism
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}:{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
such that

(i)

T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
is
\sigma
w-continuous on
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}

for all
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,

(ii)

T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\right)\circ\rho(A)
,
for all
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.

Furthermore, it has the following properties.

(a)

It is unique in the sense that if
X_{\rho}:{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
is a
*
-homomorphism which is

\sigma
w-continuous on
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}

for all
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
and
X_{\rho}\circ\pi_{0}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\right)\circ\rho(A)
,
for all
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
, then
X_{\rho}=T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
.

(b)

For any cone
\Gamma
and
V_{\rho\Gamma}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Gamma}
we have

\displaystyle T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma^{c}}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,\Gamma}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma^{c}}}.

(2.41)

In particular, the representation
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}:{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
belongs to
{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
,

(c)

For any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, we have

T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}|_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}}=T_{\rho,\Lambda}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
.

(d)

We have
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}}}=\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}}}
.

(e)

For any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, there is some
C_{\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

such that
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset\mathfrak{A}(C_{\Lambda})
.

Recall
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
from Lemma
2.4
.
We claim that

\displaystyle T_{0}(x):=T_{\rho,\Lambda}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}(x),\quad\text{if}\quad x\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime},\quad\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

(2.42)

defines a well-defined
*
-homomorphism

T_{0}:{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
To see this, suppose that
x\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
, for some

\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
Because
{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
is an upward filtering set (Lemma
A.1
)
there is a
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
such that
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\subset\Lambda
.
Note that
\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}\subset\kappa_{\Lambda_{1},\theta,\varphi},\kappa_{\Lambda_{2},\theta,\varphi}
.
Therefore, by Definition
2.13
, we have

T_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}(x)=T_{\rho,\Lambda}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}(x)=T_{\rho,\Lambda_{2}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}(x)
.
Hence the map
T_{0}:{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
is well-defined.
To see that it is a
*
-homomorphism, let
x\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
and
y\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
.
As above, there is a
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
such that
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\subset\Lambda
, and we have

x,y\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
. On
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}
,
T_{0}
is a
*
-homomorphism we see that

T_{0}
is
*
-homomorphism on
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
.
By the definition,
T_{0}
is a bounded map.
As
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
is norm-dense in
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
by Lemma
2.4
,
T_{0}
extends to a
*
-homomorphism
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}

on
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
uniquely.
From Lemma
2.12
, for each
\Lambda
, there is some

C_{\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
such that

T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}\right)=T_{0}\left(\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset\mathfrak{A}(C_{\Lambda})\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
This proves (e) and
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left({\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}\right)\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,
By the definition,
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
is clearly

\sigma
w-continuous on
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}

for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
(i). (c) holds from the above definition.
To see that
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
satisfies (ii),
note that any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm loc}
, belongs to
{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}
for some
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}(A)=T_{\rho,\Lambda}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}\right)\circ\rho(A).\end{split}

(2.43)

In the last equality, we used
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{K_{\Lambda}^{c}}
.
As this holds for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm loc}
, we obtain (ii).

Next let us prove (a).
For
X_{\rho}
in (a),

X_{\rho}
and
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
coincides on
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})
, for all
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
From the
\sigma
w-continuity of
X_{\rho}
and
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
on
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
,

X_{\rho}
and
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
coincides on
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}
, for all
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
Hence they coincides on
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
.
Because
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
is norm-dense in
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, we obtain (a).

To prove (b),
let
\Gamma
be a cone.
From (ii),

\displaystyle T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\right)\circ\rho(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho,\Gamma}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}(A)

(2.44)

for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma^{c}}
.
This proves (b).
(d) follows from (b) setting
\Gamma=\Lambda_{0}
and
\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}={V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}
.

\square

These extensions depending on
\Lambda_{0},\theta,\varphi

are related to each other as follows.

Lemma 2.15
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
We have the following.

(i)

Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,

\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
such that
\rho_{1}\simeq_{u.e.}\rho_{2}
.
Then for any

\bar{V}_{\rho_{i},\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho_{i},\Lambda_{0}}

i=1,2
, we have

T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{0}}}\simeq_{u.e.}T_{\rho_{2}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho_{2},\Lambda_{0}}}
.

(ii)

For
\theta_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi_{i}\in(0,\pi)
with

(\theta_{1}-\varphi_{1},\theta_{1}+\varphi_{1})\subset(\theta_{2}-\varphi_{2},\theta_{2}+\varphi_{2})
,
we have
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})}\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1})}
and

{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1})}
.
For any
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})}
,

\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
,
\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}
,
we have

T_{\rho}^{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})}}=T_{\rho}^{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}.

(iii)

Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,

\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
and
\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}
,
\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}
.
Then

T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{*}\right)T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}}.

(i)

Because
\rho_{1}\simeq_{u.e.}\rho_{2}
, there is a unitary
W
on
{\mathcal{H}}
such that

\rho_{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W)\rho_{1}
.
Because

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho_{2},{\tilde{\Lambda}}}W\right)\circ\rho_{1}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}^{c}}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho_{2},{\tilde{\Lambda}}}\right)\circ\rho_{2}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}^{c}}}=\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{{\tilde{\Lambda}}^{c}}},

(2.45)

we have
V_{\rho_{2},{\tilde{\Lambda}}}W\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho_{1},{\tilde{\Lambda}}}
, for any
{\tilde{\Lambda}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
and
V_{\rho_{2}\tilde{\Lambda}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho_{2}\tilde{\Lambda}}
.
Using this for
\tilde{\Lambda}:=K_{\Lambda}
with
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
and (c) of Lemma
2.12
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{0}}}|_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{0}}\left(V_{\rho_{2},K_{\Lambda}}W\right)^{*}\right)|_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{0}}W^{*}\bar{V}_{\rho_{2},\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\bar{V}_{\rho_{2},\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho_{2},K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)|_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{0}}W^{*}\bar{V}_{\rho_{2},\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)T_{\rho_{2}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho_{2},\Lambda_{0}}}|_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}},\end{split}

(2.46)

for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
and
K_{\Lambda}\in\kappa(\Lambda,\theta,\varphi)
.
Hence by Lemma
2.14
(a), we obtain

\displaystyle T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{0}}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{0}}W^{*}\bar{V}_{\rho_{2},\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)T_{\rho_{2}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho_{2},\Lambda_{0}}}.

(2.47)

(ii)
The first claim is trivial.
Note that
X_{\rho}:=T_{\rho}^{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})}}:{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})}\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
is a
*
-homomorphism

\sigma
w-continous on any
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
with
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1})}
,
satisfying
X_{\rho}\circ\pi_{0}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\right)\circ\rho
.
From Lemma
2.14
(a), we get
X_{\rho}=T_{\rho}^{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
.

(iii)

Note that

X_{\rho}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{*}\right)T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}}:{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
is a
*
-homomorphism

\sigma
w-continous on any
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
with
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

such that

\displaystyle X_{\rho}\circ\pi_{0}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{*}\right)T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}}\circ\pi_{0}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}^{*}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}\right)\rho=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}\right)\rho.

(2.48)

From Lemma
2.14
(a), we get
X_{\rho}=T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
.

\square

Lemma 2.16
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
, and
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
For any
\varepsilon>0
,
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
with
\Lambda_{0}\subset\Lambda
,

\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
and
\bar{V}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}
,
we have

\displaystyle T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime})\subset\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon}).

(2.49)

We may write
\Lambda=\Lambda_{\bm{p},\bar{\theta},\bar{\varphi}}

with
\theta+\varphi<\bar{\theta}-\bar{\varphi}<\bar{\theta}+\bar{\varphi}<\theta-\varphi+2\pi
.
Set
\varepsilon^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{4}\min\{\varepsilon,\bar{\theta}-\bar{\varphi}-\theta-\varphi\}>0
.
Then

\displaystyle K_{\Lambda}:=\Lambda_{\bm{p},\bar{\theta}-\bar{\varphi}-2\varepsilon^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}}+\left(R_{2\varepsilon^{\prime},\frac{1}{4}\min\{\varepsilon^{\prime},\bar{\theta}-\bar{\varphi}-3\varepsilon^{\prime}-\theta-\varphi\}}+1\right)\bm{e}_{\bar{\theta}-\bar{\varphi}-2\varepsilon^{\prime}}

(2.50)

belongs to
\kappa_{\Lambda,\theta,\varphi}
.
Hence, by definition, with
V_{\rho K_{\Lambda}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho K_{\Lambda}}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}|_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)|_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}}.

(2.51)

Note that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}\cup K_{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda\cup\Lambda_{\bm{p},\bar{\theta}-\bar{\varphi}-2\varepsilon^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}}\subset\Lambda_{\varepsilon}.

(2.52)

By Lemma
2.2
, from this, we have

V_{\rho\Lambda}V_{\rho K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon})
.
Hence we get

\displaystyle T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left({\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho\Lambda}V_{\rho K_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)\left({\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}}\right)\subset\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{\varepsilon}).

(2.53)

\square

Lemma 2.17
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
and
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
For any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,
\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
and
\bar{V}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}
,

T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
is
\sigma
w-continuous on
\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)
.

Let
\{x_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha}
be a net in
\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)

converging to
x\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)
in the
\sigma
w-topology.
By the approcimate Haag duality, we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}^{*})(x_{\alpha}),\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}^{*})(x)\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda-R_{|\arg\Lambda|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
,
for
\varepsilon>0
small enough.
From

\sigma w-\lim_{\alpha}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}^{*})(x_{\alpha})=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}^{*})(x)

and the
\sigma
w-continuity of
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}
on

\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda-R_{|\arg\Lambda|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
,
we have

\displaystyle\sigma w-\lim_{\alpha}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}^{*}\right)\right)\circ T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}(x_{\alpha})=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\left(U_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}^{*}\right)\right))\circ T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}(x).

(2.54)

This proves the claim.

\square

Lemma 2.18
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
.
Let
\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
,

\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,
t\geq 0
,
\varepsilon,\delta>0

with
\left(\Lambda_{1}\right)_{\varepsilon+\delta},\left(\Lambda_{2}\right)_{\varepsilon+\delta}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

and
|\arg\Lambda_{2}|+4\varepsilon<2\pi
.
Let
\bar{V}_{\rho\Lambda_{1}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho\Lambda_{1}}
.
Let
U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}
,
f_{|\arg\Lambda_{2}|,\varepsilon,\delta}(t)

be a unitary and a function
given in Definition
1.1
.
Suppose that
\left(\Lambda_{2}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)_{\varepsilon+\delta}\subset\Lambda_{1}^{c}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}}\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right)U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}^{*}-\mathbb{I}\right\|\leq 2f_{|\arg\Lambda_{2}|,\varepsilon,\delta}(t).

(2.55)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}}\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right)U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}^{*}-\mathbb{I}\right\|=\left\|T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}}\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right)-U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right\|\\
&\leq\left\|T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}}\left(\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon,\delta,t}\right)-\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon,\delta,t}\right\|+2\left\|\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon,\delta,t}-U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right\|\\
&=2\left\|\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon,\delta,t}-U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right\|\leq 2f_{|\arg\Lambda_{2}|,\varepsilon,\delta}(t),\end{split}

(2.56)

because
\left(\Lambda_{2}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)_{\varepsilon+\delta}\subset\Lambda_{1}^{c}
,
Lemma
2.14
(d) and
\sigma
w-continuity of
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}}}

on
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{2}-t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)_{\varepsilon+\delta}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
.

\square

Lemma 2.19
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

with
\arg(\Lambda_{1})\cap\arg(\Lambda_{2})=\emptyset
.
Then for any
\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
and
\bar{V}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}
,

t\geq 0

we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\left\|\left.T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\right|_{\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2})}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2})}\right\|=0.

(2.57)

Choose and fix
\varepsilon,\delta>0
such that

(\Lambda_{1})_{\varepsilon+\delta},(\Lambda_{2})_{\varepsilon+\delta}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,

|\arg\Lambda_{2}|+4\varepsilon<2\pi

and
\arg(\Lambda_{1})_{\varepsilon+\delta}\cap\arg(\Lambda_{2})_{\varepsilon+\delta}=\emptyset
.
Set

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}:=\left(\Lambda_{2}\right)_{\varepsilon}-R_{|\arg\Lambda_{2}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}.

(2.58)

By the approximate Haag duality, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2})\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right).

(2.59)

Fix any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
.
Fix
s\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle 2f_{|\arg\Lambda_{2}|,\varepsilon,\delta}(s)<\epsilon^{\prime}.

(2.60)

There is some
t_{1}\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{2},\left(\Lambda_{2}-s\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)_{\varepsilon+\delta}\subset\left(\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)^{c},

(2.61)

for all
t\geq t_{1}
.
From Lemma
2.18
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\left(U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}\right)-U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}\right\|<\epsilon^{\prime},

(2.62)

for any
t\geq t_{1}
.
Then for any
t\geq t_{1}
,

x\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2})
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}(x)-x\right\|=\left\|T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}^{*}\right)(x)-x\right\|\\
&=\left\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\left(U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}\right)\right)\circ T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}^{*}\right)(x)\right)-x\right\|\\
&=\left\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\left(U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}\right)\right)\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}^{*}\right)(x)-x\right\|\leq 2\epsilon^{\prime}\left\|x\right\|.\end{split}

(2.63)

In the third equality,
we used
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2}\varepsilon}^{*}\right)(x)\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

and

\displaystyle\left.T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\right|_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime}}.

(2.64)

The last property (
2.64
) follows from (
2.61
)
and Lemma
2.14
(d) (i).
Hence we get

\displaystyle\left\|\left.T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\right|_{\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2})}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2})}\right\|\leq 2\epsilon^{\prime}

(2.65)

for any
t\geq t_{1}
, completing the proof.

\square

Lemma 2.20
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

with
\arg(\Lambda_{1})\cap\arg(\Lambda_{2})=\emptyset
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2}),\\
y\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}),\\
\left\|x\right\|,\left\|y\right\|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\|xy-yx\right\|=0.

(2.66)

Choose and fix
\varepsilon,\delta>0
such that

(\Lambda_{1})_{\varepsilon+\delta},(\Lambda_{2})_{\varepsilon+\delta}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}

and
\arg(\Lambda_{1})_{\varepsilon+\delta}\cap\arg(\Lambda_{2})_{\varepsilon+\delta}=\emptyset
,

|\arg\Lambda_{2}|+4\varepsilon<2\pi

.
Recall the notations in Definition
1.1
.

Fix any
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
.
There is some
s\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle f_{|\arg\Lambda_{2}|,\varepsilon,\delta}(s)<\epsilon^{\prime}.

(2.67)

Choose and fix such
s
.
There is some
t_{1}\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle\left(\Lambda_{2}\right)_{\varepsilon+\delta}-s\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}},\quad\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}:=\left(\Lambda_{2}\right)_{\varepsilon}-R_{|\arg\Lambda_{2}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\subset\left(\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)^{c},

(2.68)

for all
t\geq t_{1}
.
From the approximate Haag duality, there are some unitaries

U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}

and

\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon,\delta,s}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{2}\right)_{\varepsilon+\delta}-s\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2})=\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{2}^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{2}-R_{|\arg\Lambda_{2}|,\varepsilon}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right),\\
\left\|U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}-\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon,\delta,s}\right\|\leq f_{|\arg\Lambda_{2}|,\varepsilon,\delta}(s)<\epsilon^{\prime}.\end{split}

(2.69)

Let
t\geq t_{1}
,

x\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2})
,
y\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}})
,
with
\left\|x\right\|,\left\|y\right\|\leq 1
.
By (
2.69
)

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}^{*}\right)(x)\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}}\right)^{\prime\prime},

commutes with
y
because of (
2.68
).
By (
2.68
),
y
also commutes with
\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon,\delta,s}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|xy-yx\right\|=\left\|\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}^{*}\right)(x)\right)y-y\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}\right)\left(\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}^{*}\right)(x)\right)\right\|\\
&\leq 4\left\|U_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon}-\tilde{U}_{\Lambda_{2},\varepsilon,\delta,s}\right\|<4\epsilon^{\prime}.\end{split}

(2.70)

Hence for any
t\geq t_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{2}),\\
y\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda_{1}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}),\\
\left\|x\right\|,\left\|y\right\|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\|xy-yx\right\|\leq 4\epsilon^{\prime}.

(2.71)

This completes the proof.

\square

3
The composition

From the extension of superselection sectors in the previous section, we can define the composition
of them now.

Definition 3.1
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
and

\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\eta,\Lambda_{0}}\mid\eta\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}\}
.
For any

\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
, we define

\displaystyle\rho\;\;\circ_{{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{0}}\}_{\eta}}}\;\;\sigma:=T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ T_{\sigma}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}:{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}.

(3.1)

It is a well-defined
*
-homomorphism because
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}})\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Consider Setting
2.1
and assume the approximate Haag duality.
For any
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in(0,\pi)
,

\Lambda_{i}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{i},\varphi_{i})}
,
i=1,2

\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
, and
\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{i}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\eta,\Lambda_{i}}\mid\eta\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}\}
,

i=1,2
,
we have

\displaystyle\rho\;\;\circ_{{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1}),\Lambda_{1},\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{1}}\}_{\eta}}}\;\;\sigma\simeq_{u.e.}\rho\;\;\circ_{{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2}),\Lambda_{2},\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{2}}\}_{\eta}}}\;\;\sigma

(3.2)

1.

For any
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,

\Lambda_{i}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,
i=1,2
,

\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
and
\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{i}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\eta,\Lambda_{i}}\mid\eta\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}\}
,

i=1,2
, we
show

\displaystyle\rho\;\;\circ_{{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1},\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{1}}\}_{\eta}}}\;\;\sigma\simeq_{u.e.}\rho\;\;\circ_{{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{2},\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{2}}\}_{\eta}}}\;\;\sigma.

(3.3)

Let
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
be a cone including
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}
(Lemma
A.3
).
Fix some
\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\eta,\Lambda}\mid\eta\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}\}

for this
\Lambda
.
Then, by Lemma
2.15
(iii), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho\;\;\circ_{{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{i},\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{i}}\}_{\eta}}}\;\;\sigma=T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{i},\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{i}}}\circ T_{\sigma}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{i},\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{i}}}\circ\pi_{0}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{i}}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda}^{*}\right)T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda,\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda}}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{i}}\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda}^{*}\right)T_{\sigma}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda,\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda}}\circ\pi_{0}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{i}}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda}^{*}T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda,\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda}}\left(\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{i}}\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda}^{*}\right)\right)\circ T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda,\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda}}T_{\sigma}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda,\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda}}\circ\pi_{0}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{i}}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda}^{*}T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda,\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda}}\left(\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{i}}\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda}^{*}\right)\right)\circ\rho\;\;\circ_{{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda,\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda}\}_{\eta}}}\;\;\sigma,\end{split}

(3.4)

for
i=1,2
.
In the third equality, we used the fact
\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{i}}\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda}^{*}\in\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
(Lemma
2.2
).
Hence we get (
3.3
).

2.

For
\theta_{i}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi_{i}\in(0,\pi)
with

(\theta_{1}-\varphi_{1},\theta_{1}+\varphi_{1})\subset(\theta_{2}-\varphi_{2},\theta_{2}+\varphi_{2})
,

\Lambda_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})}\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1})}
,

\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
, and
\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{0}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\eta,\Lambda_{0}}\mid\eta\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}\}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho\;\;\circ_{{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2}),\Lambda_{0},\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{0}}\}_{\eta}}}\;\;\sigma=T_{\rho}^{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ T_{\sigma}^{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}=T_{\rho}^{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ T_{\sigma}^{(\theta_{2},\varphi_{2})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}\\
&=T_{\rho}^{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ T_{\sigma}^{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1})\Lambda_{0}\bar{V}_{\sigma,\Lambda_{0}}}\circ\pi_{0}=\rho\;\;\circ_{{(\theta_{1},\varphi_{1}),\Lambda_{0},\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{0}}\}_{\eta}}}\;\;\sigma.\end{split}

(3.5)

by Lemma
2.15
(ii).

3.

Let
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in(0,\pi)
,

\Lambda_{i}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta_{i},\varphi_{i})}
,
i=1,2
,

\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}
and
\{\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{i}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\eta,\Lambda_{i}}\mid\eta\in{\mathcal{O}}_{0}\}
,

i=1,2
.
Because
