\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}

is properly infinite.
This requires different arguments than those in AQFT,
because
\Omega
is not cyclic for
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}
.
We use the gap condition.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\Phi
be a uniformly bounded finite range interaction on
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

and
\tau_{\Phi}
the
C^{*}
-dynamics given by
\Phi
.
Let
\omega
be a pure
\tau_{\Phi}
-ground state satisfying the gap condition:
there is a
\gamma>0
such that

\displaystyle-i\omega\left(B^{*}\delta_{\Phi}(B)\right)\geq\gamma\omega(B^{*}B),

(5.3)

for any
B\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
in the domain of the generator
\delta_{\Phi}
of
\tau_{\Phi}
with
\omega(B)=0
.
Let
({\mathcal{H}},\pi_{0},\Omega)
be a GNS representation of
\omega
.
Then for any cone
\Lambda
in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
,
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}
does not have
any normal tracial state.

Suppose that there is a cone
\Lambda
such that
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}
has
a normal tracial state
\tau
.
We derive a contradiction out of it.

For any
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
, there are unit vectors
\xi_{x},\eta_{x}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{d}

such that

\displaystyle\omega\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)=0,\quad\omega\left(X_{\xi_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\geq\frac{1}{d}.

(5.4)

Here
X_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}^{(x)}
denotes an element in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\{x\}}\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{M}}\nolimits_{d}

such that
X_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}^{(x)}\zeta=\left\langle\zeta_{2},\zeta\right\rangle\zeta_{1}
.
To see this, let
\{e_{ij}^{(x)}\}
be a system of matrix units in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\{x\}}
.
Because
\sum_{i=1}^{d}\omega(e_{ii}^{(x)})=1
,
there is
i=1,\ldots,d
such that
\omega(e_{ii}^{(x)})\geq\frac{1}{d}
.
We set
\xi_{x}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{d}
a unit vector corresponding to this projection

e_{ii}^{(x)}
, i.e.,
X_{\xi_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}=e_{ii}^{(x)}
.
Let
\eta_{x}
be a unit vector perpendicular to
D\xi_{x}
,
where
D
is the reduced density matrix of
\omega
onto
{\mathcal{A}}_{\{x\}}
.
Then we see these vectors satisfy the condition (
5.4
).

We also note that because our interaction is uniformly bounded and finite range,
there is some
c_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\delta_{\Phi}(B)\right\|\leq c_{1}\left\|B\right\|,\quad\text{for all}\quad x\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2},\quad B\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\{x\}}.

(5.5)

From now on, we fix
0<\varepsilon<\frac{\gamma}{4c_{1}d}
.

Note that because
\omega
is pure,
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is a factor.
To see this, note that

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}\cap\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime}\;\;\subset\;\;\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}\cap\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime}\;\;\subset\;\;\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}})^{\prime}={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}.

(5.6)

By definition,
\tau\circ\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}}
is a
\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}}
-normal state.
Because
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is a factor, this means that
\tau\circ\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}}
and
\omega|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}}

are quasi-equivalent.
From Corollary 2.6.11 of
[
BR1
]
, this means
there exists a finite subset
S
of
\Lambda\cap{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}

such that

\displaystyle\left|\tau\circ\pi_{0}(B)-\omega(B)\right|<\varepsilon\left\|B\right\|,\quad B\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\setminus S},

(5.7)

for our fixed
\varepsilon
.

Suppose that
\Phi
has a range less than
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Let
S_{m}
be a set of points in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}

whose distance from
S\cup\Lambda^{c}
is less than or equal to
m
.
Let
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\setminus\left(S_{m}\right)
.
For this
x
,
X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)},\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}
belongs to the domain of
\delta_{\Phi}

and
\omega\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)=\omega\left(\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\right)=0
.
Therefore, by the gap condition (
5.3
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-i\omega\left(\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\right)+i\omega\left(\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\right)\\
&=-i\omega\left(\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\right)-i\omega\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\right)\\
&\geq\gamma\omega\left(\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)+\gamma\omega\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\right)\geq\gamma\omega\left(X_{\xi_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\geq\frac{\gamma}{d}.\end{split}

(5.8)

In the equality, we used the fact that
\omega
is
\tau_{\Phi}
-invariant.
On the other hand,
by tracial property of
\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|-i\omega\left(\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\right)+i\omega\left(\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\right)\right|\\
&\leq\left|-i\omega\left(\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\right)+i\tau\circ\pi_{0}\left(\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\right)\right|\\
&+\left|-i\tau\circ\pi_{0}\left(\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\right)+i\tau\circ\pi_{0}\left(\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\right)\right|\\
&+\left|-i\tau\circ\pi_{0}\left(\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\right)+i\omega\left(\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\right)\right|\\
&\leq\varepsilon\left\|\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\right\|+\varepsilon\left\|\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)^{*}\right\|\\
&\leq 2\varepsilon c_{1}.\end{split}

(5.9)

Note that
\delta_{\Phi}\left(X_{\eta_{x},\xi_{x}}^{(x)}\right)
belongs to

{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda\setminus S}
because of our choice of
x\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}\setminus S_{m}
.
We applied (
5.7
).
Combining (
5.8
) and (
5.9
), we obtain

\frac{\gamma}{d}\leq 2\varepsilon c_{1}
, which contradicts to
our choice of
\varepsilon
.

\square

The following Theorem is proven in
[
NO
]
.

Theorem 5.4
.

Consider Setting
2.1
. Suppose that

\pi_{0}
has a nontrivial sector theory.
Then for any cone
\Lambda
,
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is not of type
I
.

Lemma 5.5
.

In Setting
5.1
,
for any cone
\Lambda
,
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is either type
II_{\infty}

or type
III
factor.
Furthermore,
\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)=\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}

is either type
II_{\infty}

or type
III
factor.

Let
\Lambda
be a cone.
Recall from the proof of Lemma
5.3
that
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is a factor.
By Corollary 1.20 V of
[
T1
]
,

\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
should be either type
I
, type
II_{1}
, type
II_{\infty}
or type
III
factor.
From Theorem
5.4
, it is not of type
I
.
From Lemma
5.3
,
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
does not have a normal tracial state.
From Theorem 2.4 V
[
T1
]
, this means that
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is not finite.
Therefore, it is not type
II_{1}
.

Because
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime\prime}
is factor,
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}
is factor.
By Corollary 2.24 V of
[
T1
]
,
\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)=\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}

is not type
I
because
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime\prime}
is not.
If
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}
is type
II_{1}
,
it admits sufficiently many finite normal trace. (Theorem 2.4 V
[
T1
]
.)
From

\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}\subset\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}
, this means
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
admits sufficiently many finite normal trace, which is not true because

\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is not finite.
Therefore,
\mathfrak{A}(\Lambda)=\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}})^{\prime}
is
either type
II_{\infty}

or type
III
factor.

\square

Lemma 5.6
.

For any
\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
, we have

\displaystyle(\rho,\sigma)\subset\left(T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\mathbb{I}},T_{\sigma}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\mathbb{I}}\right)\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}.

(5.10)

For any
R\in(\rho,\sigma)
, we have

\displaystyle RT_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\mathbb{I}}\left(\pi_{0}(A)\right)=R\rho(A)=\sigma(A)R=T_{\sigma}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\mathbb{I}}\left(\pi_{0}(A)\right)R,

(5.11)

for all
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

using Lemma
2.14
(ii) with
\bar{V}_{\rho\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
.
By the
\sigma
w-continuity of
T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\mathbb{I}}
,
T_{\sigma}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\mathbb{I}}
on

\pi_{0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}
this is extended to
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
of Lemma
2.4
,
and from
Lemma
2.4
, this extends to
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
The last inclusion is by Lemma
4.2
.

\square

Lemma 5.7
.

Consider Setting
5.1
.
For any
\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
,
there exists a
\tau\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
,
and isometries
u\in(\rho,\tau)
,

v\in(\sigma,\tau)
such that
uu^{*}+vv^{*}=\mathbb{I}
.

From Lemma
5.5
,
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}
is properly intinite for any cone
\Lambda
.
Therefore, there exist a projection
p_{\Lambda}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}

such that
p_{\Lambda}\sim\mathbb{I}-p_{\Lambda}\sim\mathbb{I}

from Proposition 1.36 V
[
T1
]
, i.e.,
there are isometries
u_{\Lambda},v_{\Lambda}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}

such that
u_{\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}=p_{\Lambda}
and
v_{\Lambda}v_{\Lambda}^{*}=\mathbb{I}-p_{\Lambda}
.
Define
\tau:{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}_{0})
by

\displaystyle\tau(A):=u_{\Lambda_{0}}\rho(A)u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}+v_{\Lambda_{0}}\sigma(A)v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*},\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}.

(5.12)

Because the range of
u_{\Lambda_{0}}
and
v_{\Lambda_{0}}
are orthogonal
and they are isometries,
\tau
is a
*
-homomorphism.

To see
\tau\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
, fix some

V_{\rho,\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda}
and

V_{\sigma,\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma,\Lambda}

for each cone
\Lambda
.
For
\Lambda_{0}
, we may and we do set
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
,
V_{\sigma,\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
, because

\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
.
Set

\displaystyle W_{\Lambda}:=u_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Lambda}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}+v_{\Lambda}V_{\sigma\Lambda}v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}.

(5.13)

By our choice
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
,
V_{\sigma,\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
,
we have
W_{\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
.
For general
\Lambda
,
W_{\Lambda}
is a unitary:

\displaystyle W_{\Lambda}^{*}W_{\Lambda}=u_{\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Lambda}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}+v_{\Lambda_{0}}V_{\sigma\Lambda}^{*}v_{\Lambda}^{*}v_{\Lambda}V_{\sigma\Lambda}v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}=u_{\Lambda_{0}}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}+v_{\Lambda_{0}}v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}=\mathbb{I},

(5.14)

\displaystyle W_{\Lambda}W_{\Lambda}^{*}=u_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}+v_{\Lambda}V_{\sigma\Lambda}^{*}v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}v_{\Lambda}V_{\sigma\Lambda_{0}}v_{\Lambda}^{*}=u_{\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}+v_{\Lambda}v_{\Lambda}^{*}=\mathbb{I}.

(5.15)

For any cone
\Lambda
, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}\right)\circ\tau|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}=\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}
.
In fact, for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}\right)\circ\tau(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Lambda}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}+v_{\Lambda}V_{\sigma\Lambda}v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)\left(u_{\Lambda_{0}}\rho(A)u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}+v_{\Lambda_{0}}\sigma(A)v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Lambda}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)\left(u_{\Lambda_{0}}\rho(A)u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)+\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\Lambda}V_{\sigma\Lambda}v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)\left(v_{\Lambda_{0}}\sigma(A)v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Lambda}\right)\left(\rho(A)\right)+\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\Lambda}V_{\sigma\Lambda}\right)\left(\sigma(A)\right)\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\Lambda}\right)\left(\pi_{0}(A)\right)+\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\Lambda}\right)\left(\pi_{0}(A)\right)\\
&=\left(u_{\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}+v_{\Lambda}v_{\Lambda}^{*}\right)\left(\pi_{0}(A)\right)=\pi_{0}(A).\end{split}

(5.16)

In the fourth equality, we used
u_{\Lambda},v_{\Lambda}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}

and
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}
.
In particular,
because
W_{\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
, we have
\tau|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}}}=\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}}}
.
This proves
\tau\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
.

We have
u_{\Lambda_{0}}\in(\rho,\tau)
because

\displaystyle\tau(A)u_{\Lambda_{0}}=\left(u_{\Lambda_{0}}\rho(A)u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}+v_{\Lambda_{0}}\sigma(A)v_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)u_{\Lambda_{0}}=u_{\Lambda_{0}}\rho(A)u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}=u_{\Lambda_{0}}\rho(A)

(5.17)

for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.
Similarly,
v_{\Lambda_{0}}\in(\sigma,\tau)
.
Setting
u:=u_{\Lambda_{0}}
and
v:=v_{\Lambda_{0}}

we obtain the isometries satisfying the required condition.

\square

Lemma 5.8
.

Consider Setting
5.1
.
For any
\rho\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
and a nonzero projection
p\in(\rho,\rho)
,
there exists a
\tau\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}

and an isometry
v\in(\tau,\rho)
,
such that
vv^{*}=p
.

In order to prove this, we first prepare following general Lemmas.
The following Lemma is well known, and can be derived as a refinement of the proof of Lemma 2.5.2
of
[
L
]
.

Lemma 5.9
.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta_{\varepsilon}>0

satisfying the following.:
For any unital
C^{*}
-algebra
\mathfrak{B}
and projections
p,q\in\mathfrak{B}

such that
\left\|q-qp\right\|<\delta_{\varepsilon}
,
there is a partial isometry
w\in\mathfrak{B}
such that

\displaystyle ww^{*}=q,\quad w^{*}w\leq p,\quad\left\|w-q\right\|<\varepsilon.

(5.18)

Lemma 5.10
.

There is some
\frac{1}{2}>\delta>0
satisfying the following.:
Let
{\mathcal{H}}
be a separable Hilbert space, and
{\mathcal{N}},{\mathcal{M}}

be infinite factors on
{\mathcal{H}}
such that
{\mathcal{N}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}
.
Let
p
be a nonzero projection in
{\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}
, and
u
a unitary on
{\mathcal{H}}

such that
\tilde{p}:=upu^{*}\in{\mathcal{M}}
and
\left\|u-\mathbb{I}\right\|<\delta
.
Then
\tilde{p}
is equivalent to
\mathbb{I}
in
{\mathcal{M}}
.

We set
\delta:=\frac{1}{8}\cdot\min\left\{\delta_{\frac{1}{8}},\frac{1}{8}\right\}
,
with
\delta_{\varepsilon}
in the Lemma
5.8
.
Let us consider
{\mathcal{H}},{\mathcal{N}},{\mathcal{M}},p,u
as above, satisfying the condition for this
\delta
.
We would like to show that
\tilde{p}:=upu^{*}
is equivalent to
\mathbb{I}
in
{\mathcal{M}}
.
If
p=\mathbb{I}
, this is trivial. We assume
p\neq\mathbb{I}
.

Because
{\mathcal{N}}
is an infinite factor,
\mathbb{I}
is properly infinite in
{\mathcal{N}}
.
Therefore, from Proposition 1.36 V
[
T1
]
, there exists a projection
F
in
{\mathcal{N}}
and an isometry

v\in{\mathcal{N}}

such that
\mathbb{I}\neq F
and
vv^{*}=F
.
As
p\in{\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}
is a nonzero projection and
{\mathcal{N}}
is a factor,
the map

\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}\ni x\mapsto xp\in{\mathcal{N}}p

(5.19)

is a
*
-isomorphim.
Therefore,
vv^{*}p=Fp\neq p
.
Because
F,v\in{\mathcal{N}}
and
p\in{\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}
,
p-vpv^{*}=p(1-vv^{*})=p(1-F)
is a nonzero projection.
In particular, we have
\left\|p-vpv^{*}\right\|=1
.
Because
(v\tilde{p})^{*}v\tilde{p}=\tilde{p}v^{*}v\tilde{p}=\tilde{p}
,

v\tilde{p}
is a partial isometry in
{\mathcal{M}}
and
v\tilde{p}v^{*}
is a projection in

{\mathcal{M}}
.
Because
\left\|u-\mathbb{I}\right\|<\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|v\tilde{p}v^{*}(\mathbb{I}-\tilde{p})\right\|\leq 2\left\|\tilde{p}-p\right\|+\left\|vpv^{*}(\mathbb{I}-p)\right\|\leq 4\left\|u-\mathbb{I}\right\|<\delta_{\frac{1}{8}}.

(5.20)

Therefore, from Lemma
5.9
, there exists a partial isometry
w\in{\mathcal{M}}

such that
ww^{*}=v\tilde{p}v^{*}
,
w^{*}w\leq\tilde{p}

and
\left\|w-v\tilde{p}v^{*}\right\|<\frac{1}{8}
.
Set
w_{0}:=w^{*}v\tilde{p}\in{\mathcal{M}}
.
We have

\displaystyle w_{0}^{*}w_{0}=\tilde{p}v^{*}ww^{*}v\tilde{p}=\tilde{p}v^{*}v\tilde{p}v^{*}v\tilde{p}=\tilde{p},

(5.21)

and
w_{0}
is a partial isometry.
We also have

\displaystyle w_{0}w_{0}^{*}=w^{*}v\tilde{p}v^{*}w\leq w^{*}w\leq\tilde{p}.

(5.22)

Therefore, if
\tilde{p}-w_{0}w_{0}^{*}\neq 0
, then

\tilde{p}
is infinite.
In fact,
because

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|w_{0}w_{0}^{*}-\tilde{p}\right\|=\left\|w^{*}v\tilde{p}v^{*}w-\tilde{p}\right\|=\left\|\left(w-v\tilde{p}v^{*}\right)^{*}v\tilde{p}v^{*}w+v\tilde{p}v^{*}(w-v\tilde{p}v^{*})+v\tilde{p}v^{*}-\tilde{p}\right\|\\
\geq\left\|v\tilde{p}v^{*}-\tilde{p}\right\|-2\left\|w-v\tilde{p}v^{*}\right\|\geq\left\|vpv^{*}-p\right\|-4\left\|u-\mathbb{I}\right\|-2\left\|w-v\tilde{p}v^{*}\right\|\geq\frac{1}{2},\end{split}

(5.23)

we have
\tilde{p}-w_{0}w_{0}^{*}\neq 0
, and

\tilde{p}
is infinite.
We used
\left\|p-vpv^{*}\right\|=1
in the last inequality.
Because
{\mathcal{H}}
is separable and
{\mathcal{M}}
is an infinite factor, from Corollary 6.3.5 of

[
KR
]
,
\tilde{p}
is equivalent to
\mathbb{I}
in
{\mathcal{M}}
.

\square

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
{\mathcal{N}},{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{R}}
be von Neumann algebras
acting on a Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}
such that
{\mathcal{N}},{\mathcal{M}}\subset{\mathcal{R}}
and

{\mathcal{N}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
.
Suppose that
{\mathcal{M}}
is an infinite factor.
Then any nonzero projection in
{\mathcal{N}}
is infinite in
{\mathcal{R}}
.

Let
p\in{\mathcal{N}}
be a nonzero projection.
Since
{\mathcal{M}}
is a factor, and
p\in{\mathcal{N}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
a projection,
the map

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}\ni a\mapsto ap\in{\mathcal{M}}p

(5.24)

is a
*
-isomorphism.

Because
{\mathcal{M}}
is an infinite factor, by Proposition 1.36 V
[
T1
]
,
there is a projection
q\in{\mathcal{M}}
such that

q\sim(\mathbb{I}-q)\sim\mathbb{I}
in
{\mathcal{M}}
.
Hence there is some
v\in{\mathcal{M}}
such that
v^{*}v=\mathbb{I}

and
vv^{*}=q
.
For this
q\in{\mathcal{M}}
,
pq\in{\mathcal{R}}
is a projection.
Because of the injectivity of the map (
5.24
), we have

p\neq pq
.
Set
w:=pv\in{\mathcal{R}}
.
We have

\displaystyle ww^{*}=pvv^{*}p=pqp=pq,\quad w^{*}w=v^{*}pv=v^{*}vp=p.

(5.25)

Hence
p
and
pq
are equivalent in
{\mathcal{R}}
.
Because
p\neq pq
,
p
is infinite in
{\mathcal{R}}
.

\square

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
{\mathcal{N}},{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{R}}
be von Neumann algebras acting on a separable Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}

such that
{\mathcal{N}},{\mathcal{M}}\subset{\mathcal{R}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
.
Suppose that
{\mathcal{M}}
is an infinite factor and
{\mathcal{R}}
is a factor.
Let
w
be a unitary on
{\mathcal{H}}
and
u\in{\mathcal{N}}
an isometry
such that
w^{*}uu^{*}w\in{\mathcal{N}}
.
Then there is a unitary
W
on
{\mathcal{H}}

such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(Wu^{*}\right)(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u^{*}w\right)(x),\quad x\in{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}.

(5.26)

From Lemma
5.11
,
non-zero projections
w^{*}uu^{*}w,uu^{*}\in{\mathcal{N}}

are infinite in
{\mathcal{R}}
.
Because
{\mathcal{R}}
is a factor and
{\mathcal{H}}
is separable,
from Corollary 6.3.5
[
KR
]
,

w^{*}uu^{*}w
and
uu^{*}
are equivalent in
{\mathcal{R}}
.
Namely, there is some
a\in{\mathcal{R}}
such that
w^{*}uu^{*}w=a^{*}a

and
uu^{*}=aa^{*}
.

Set
W:=u^{*}wa^{*}u
. This
W
is unitary because

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{*}W=u^{*}aw^{*}uu^{*}wa^{*}u=u^{*}aa^{*}aa^{*}u=u^{*}u=\mathbb{I},\\
WW^{*}=u^{*}wa^{*}uu^{*}aw^{*}u=u^{*}wa^{*}aa^{*}aw^{*}u=u^{*}ww^{*}u=u^{*}u=\mathbb{I}.\end{split}

(5.27)

For any
x\in{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(Wu^{*}\right)(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u^{*}wa^{*}uu^{*}\right)(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u^{*}wa^{*}aa^{*}\right)(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u^{*}wa^{*}\right)(x)\\
=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u^{*}w\right)\left(a^{*}xa\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u^{*}w\right)\left(a^{*}ax\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u^{*}w\right)(x).\end{split}

(5.28)

\square

For each cone
\Lambda
, we fix some
V_{\rho,\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho,\Lambda}
.
For
\Lambda_{0}
, we may and we do set
V_{\rho,\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}
.
Set
p_{\Lambda}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(V_{\rho,\Lambda})(p)
.
Note that
p_{\Lambda}
belongs to
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime}
for each cone
\Lambda
, because

\displaystyle p\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho\Lambda}^{*}\right)\left(\pi_{0}(A)\right)=p\rho(A)=\rho(A)p=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(V_{\rho\Lambda}^{*}\right)\left(\pi_{0}(A)\right)\cdot p,\quad A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}.

(5.29)

Let
\delta>0
be the number given in Lemma
5.10
.
For each
\varphi\in(0,2\pi)
,
\varepsilon>0
with
\varphi+8\varepsilon<2\pi
,
fix some
t_{\varphi,\varepsilon}\geq R_{\varphi,\varepsilon}

such that
f_{\varphi,\varepsilon,\varepsilon}(t_{\varphi,\epsilon})<\delta
.
(Recall the definition of approximate Haag duality Definition
1.1
.)
For each cone
\Lambda
, fix some
0<\varepsilon_{\Lambda}<\min\{\frac{2\pi-|\arg\Lambda|}{16},\frac{|\arg\Lambda|}{16}\}

and
set

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\Lambda}:=\Lambda_{-4\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}+t_{|\arg\Lambda|-8\varepsilon_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}.

(5.30)

We choose a cone
D_{\Lambda}
so that

D_{\Lambda}\subset\left(\Gamma_{\Lambda}-R_{|\arg\Gamma_{\Lambda}|,\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\bm{e}_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\right)_{\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\cap\Gamma_{\Lambda}^{c}
.
Note that

\displaystyle D_{\Lambda}\subset\left(\Gamma_{\Lambda}-R_{|\arg\Gamma_{\Lambda}|,\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\bm{e}_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\right)_{\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\cap\Gamma_{\Lambda}^{c}\subset\Lambda_{-3\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}+\left(t_{|\arg\Lambda|-8\varepsilon_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}-R_{|\arg\Gamma_{\Lambda}|,\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\right)\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda_{-3\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}.

(5.31)

Note that
|\arg\Gamma_{\Lambda}|=|\arg\Lambda|-8\varepsilon_{\Lambda}
.
By the approximate Haag duality,
there is a unitary

U_{\Gamma_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}})

satisfying

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(U_{\Gamma_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Gamma_{\Lambda}-R_{|\arg\Gamma_{\Lambda}|,\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\bm{e}_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\right)_{\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right).

(5.32)

Furthermore, there is a unitary

\displaystyle\hat{U}_{\Lambda}:=\tilde{U}_{\Gamma_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda},t_{|\arg\Lambda|-8\varepsilon_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{(\Gamma_{\Lambda})_{2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}-t_{|\arg\Lambda|-8\varepsilon_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}=\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

(5.33)

satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|U_{\Gamma_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}-\hat{U}_{\Lambda}\right\|\leq f_{\arg|\Gamma_{\Lambda}|,\varepsilon_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\left(t_{|\arg\Lambda|-8\varepsilon_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\right)<\delta<\frac{1}{2}.

(5.34)

Now we apply Lemma
5.10

to the separable (because
{\mathcal{H}}
is a GNS Hilbert space of a state on
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
) Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}
,
infinite factors

{\mathcal{N}}:=\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{D_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

{\mathcal{M}}:=\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
,

p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{D_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime}={\mathcal{N}}^{\prime}
,

u:=\hat{U}_{\Lambda}U_{\Gamma_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}^{*}\in{\mathcal{U}}({\mathcal{H}})
.
Note that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\hat{U}_{\Lambda}U_{\Gamma_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)(p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}})\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\hat{U}_{\Lambda}\right)\left(\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Gamma_{\Lambda}\right)_{\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}-R_{|\arg\Gamma_{\Lambda}|,\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\bm{e}_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}}}\right)\right)^{\prime\prime}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{M}}.

(5.35)

Applying Lemma
5.10
, we conclude that

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\hat{U}_{\Lambda}U_{\Gamma_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}^{*}\right)(p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}})
is equivalent to
\mathbb{I}
in
\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence for each cone
\Lambda
,
there is an isometry
u_{\Lambda}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}

such that
u_{\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(w_{\Lambda}\right)(p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}})
,
where we set
w_{\Lambda}:=\hat{U}_{\Lambda}U_{\Gamma_{\Lambda},\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}^{*}
.
Note that
\left\|w_{\Lambda}-\mathbb{I}\right\|<\frac{1}{2}
from (
5.34
).

For each cone
\Lambda
, we set

\displaystyle T_{\Lambda}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\right)\circ\rho:{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}}).

(5.36)

It is a
*
-homomorphism
because

\displaystyle\begin{split}&T_{\Lambda}(A)T_{\Lambda}(B)=u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\rho(A)V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}^{*}w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\rho(B)V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}^{*}w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}\\
&=u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\rho(A)V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}^{*}p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\rho(B)V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}^{*}w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}\\
&=u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\rho(A)p\rho(B)V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}^{*}w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}\\
&=u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\rho(AB)pV_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}^{*}w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}\\
&=u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\rho(AB)V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}^{*}w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}=T_{\Lambda}(AB),\end{split}

(5.37)

for any
A,B\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
.

For any cone
\Lambda
,
there is a unitary
W_{\Lambda}
such that

\displaystyle T_{\Lambda}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{\Lambda})\circ\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}.

(5.38)

To see this,
we choose a cone
C_{\Lambda}
such that

\displaystyle C_{\Lambda}\subset\left(\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\right)^{c}\cap\Lambda.

(5.39)

We use Lemma
5.12
for infinite factors

{\mathcal{N}}:=\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime}
,

{\mathcal{M}}:=\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{C_{\Lambda}}\right)^{\prime\prime}
,

{\mathcal{R}}:=\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime}

acting on the separable Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}

and a unitary
w_{\Lambda}
on
{\mathcal{H}}
and an isometry
u_{\Lambda}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime}={\mathcal{N}}
.
Because of (
5.39
), we have
{\mathcal{N}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}

and
{\mathcal{N}},{\mathcal{M}}\subset{\mathcal{R}}
.
We have

\displaystyle w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}=p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime}.

(5.40)

Applying Lemma
5.12
, there is a unitary
W_{\Lambda}

on
{\mathcal{H}}
such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}\right)(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}\right)(x),\quad x\in{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}=\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime\prime}.

(5.41)

Note that for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{\Lambda}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\right)\circ\rho(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho\Lambda}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}\right)\circ\pi_{0}(A)\\
=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}\right)\circ\pi_{0}(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}\right)\left(u_{\Lambda}^{*}\pi_{0}(A)u_{\Lambda}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}\right)\left(u_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}\pi_{0}(A)\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}\right)\left(\pi_{0}(A)\right).\end{split}

(5.42)

Here we used that

\displaystyle V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho\Lambda}^{*}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime}

(5.43)

in the third equality and (
5.41
) in the fourth equality
and the fact
u_{\Lambda}\in\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{-2\varepsilon_{\Lambda}}\right)^{c}}\right)^{\prime}\subset\pi_{0}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime}
in the sixth equality.
This proves (
5.38
).

Now we set

\displaystyle\tau:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}\right)\circ T_{\Lambda_{0}}

(5.44)

with
W_{\Lambda}
in (
5.38
).
By definition,
\tau
is a
*
-representation and we have
\tau|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}}}=\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{0}^{c}}}
by (
5.38
).
For each cone
\Lambda
, set

\displaystyle X_{\Lambda}:=W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}.

(5.45)

We have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&X_{\Lambda}X_{\Lambda}^{*}=W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}^{*}w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}W_{\Lambda}\\
&=W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}^{*}w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}W_{\Lambda}\\
&=W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}}w_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}W_{\Lambda}=W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}u_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}W_{\Lambda}=\mathbb{I}.\end{split}

(5.46)

Similarly we have
X_{\Lambda}^{*}X_{\Lambda}=\mathbb{I}

and
X_{\Lambda}
is a unitary.
From the definition, we see that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(X_{\Lambda})\circ\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{\Lambda}^{*})T_{\Lambda},

(5.47)

because

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(X_{\Lambda})\circ\tau=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}\right)\circ\rho\\
=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}\right)\circ\rho=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}p\right)\circ\rho\\
=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\right)\circ\rho=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\Lambda}^{*}u_{\Lambda}^{*}w_{\Lambda}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda}}\right)\circ\rho=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(W_{\Lambda}^{*})T_{\Lambda}.\end{split}

(5.48)

Therefore, from (
5.38
), we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits(X_{\Lambda})\circ\tau|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}=\pi_{0}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}
.
Hence we conclude
\tau\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
.

Finally, we set

\displaystyle v:=V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}

(5.49)

and prove

\displaystyle v\in(\tau,\rho),\quad v^{*}v=\mathbb{I},\quad vv^{*}=p.

(5.50)

For any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}v\tau(A)=V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{{\Lambda_{0}}}}\rho(A)V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}\\
=\rho(A)pV_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}=\rho(A)V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}p_{\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}=\rho(A)V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}=\rho(A)v.\end{split}

(5.51)

Hence
v\in(\tau,\rho)
.
Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&vv^{*}=V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}w_{\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}=V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}^{*}p_{\Lambda_{0}}V_{\rho\Gamma_{\Lambda_{0}}}=p,\\
&v^{*}v=W_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}^{*}u_{\Lambda_{0}}W_{\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}.\end{split}

(5.52)

This completes the proof.

\square

We prove that
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
and its morphisms form a braided
C^{*}
-tensor
category
[
NT
]
.

For any
\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
,

(\rho,\sigma)
is a Banach space.
For any
\rho,\sigma,\gamma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
,
the map

\displaystyle(\sigma,\gamma)\times(\rho,\sigma)\ni(S,R)\mapsto SR\in(\rho,\gamma)

(5.53)

is bilinear and
\left\|SR\right\|\leq\left\|S\right\|\left\|R\right\|
holds.
For any
R\in(\rho,\sigma)
, its adjoint
R^{*}
belongs to

(\sigma,\rho)
and
R^{**}=R
,

\left\|R^{*}R\right\|=\left\|R\right\|^{2}

(in particular,
(\rho,\rho)
is a
C^{*}
-algebra), and
R^{*}R\in(\rho,\rho)
is positive.
We define the tensor product of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
by

\displaystyle\rho\otimes\sigma:=\rho\circ_{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\{V_{\eta\Lambda_{0}}=\mathbb{I}\}}\sigma,\quad\rho,\sigma\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}.

(5.54)

Tensor product of intertwiners are given by

\displaystyle R_{1}\otimes R_{2}=R_{1}T_{\rho}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\mathbb{I}}(R_{2}),\quad R_{1}\in(\rho,\rho^{\prime}),\quad R_{2}\in(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}),\quad\rho,\rho^{\prime},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}

(5.55)

as in Lemma
4.3
.
Note that it is bi-linear.
Morphisms
\alpha_{\rho,\sigma,\gamma}:=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})}

play the role of the associativity morphisms.
(Recall Lemma
3.6
.)
The irreducible representation
\pi_{0}
corresponds to the unit object, because

T_{\pi_{0}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0},\mathbb{I}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits
.
(This can be see from Lemma
2.14
(a).)
Furthermore, setting

\lambda_{\sigma}=\rho_{\sigma}=\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})}
,
we have natural unitary isomorphisms

\lambda_{\sigma}:\pi_{o}\otimes\sigma\to\sigma

and
\rho_{\sigma}:\sigma\otimes\pi_{0}\to\sigma
.
It is clear that the associativity morphism satisfies the pentagonal diagram,
and the natural unitary isomorphisms satisfy the triangle diagram.
By Lemma
4.3
,
morphisms satisfy
(R\otimes S)^{*}=R^{*}\otimes S^{*}
.
The existence of the direct sums and subobjects are proven in Lemma
5.7
and
Lemma
5.8
respectively.
The unit object
\pi_{0}
is simple, i.e.
(\pi_{0},\pi_{0})={\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{I}_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})}

because it is irreducible.
As a subset of
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\times{\mathcal{H}}\times{\mathcal{H}}
,
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Lambda_{0}}
is a set.
Definition
4.11
and Lemma
4.16
proves that

\epsilon_{+}^{\Lambda_{0}}
gives the braiding.

\square

6
Stability of the braiding structure

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}
be uniformly bounded finite range interactions on
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

and
\omega_{\Phi_{i}}

i=1,2
pure
\tau_{\Phi_{i}}
-ground states satisfying the gap condition.
Suppose that the GNS representation of
\omega_{\Phi_{1}}
has a non-trivial sector theory and
satisfies the approximate Haag duality.
Suppose that there is a approximately-factorizable automorphism
\alpha

such that
\omega_{\Phi_{2}}=\omega_{\Phi_{1}}\circ\alpha
.
Then the following hold.

(i)

The GNS representation of
\omega_{\Phi_{2}}
has a non-trivial sector theory and
satisfies the approximate Haag duality.

(ii)

Fix some
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\varphi\in(0,\pi)

and
\Lambda_{0}^{(i)}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, for
i=1,2
.
From (i) and Theorem
5.1
, we obtain braided
C^{*}
-tensor categories
C_{1}=({\mathcal{O}}_{1},Mor({\mathcal{O}}_{1}))
,

C_{2}=({\mathcal{O}}_{2},Mor({\mathcal{O}}_{2}))
associated
to
\omega_{\Phi_{1}}

\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}
and
\omega_{\Phi_{2}}
,
\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}
respectively.
The braided
C^{*}
-tensor category
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
are unitarily monoidaly equivalent.

(i) is from
[
NO
]
and Lemma
1.3
. We prove (ii).
Let
({\mathcal{H}}_{1},\pi_{1})
be a GNS representation for
\omega_{\Phi_{1}}
.
Then
({\mathcal{H}}_{2},\pi_{2}):=({\mathcal{H}}_{1},\pi_{1}\alpha)
is a GNS representation of
\omega_{\Phi_{2}}
.
For any cone
\Lambda
and
\varepsilon>0
,
there are automorphism
\beta_{\Lambda},\tilde{\beta}_{\Lambda}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)
,

\beta_{\Lambda^{c}},\tilde{\beta}_{\Lambda^{c}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)

and
\Xi_{\Lambda,\varepsilon},\tilde{\Xi}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\cap((\Lambda^{c})_{\varepsilon})}\right)

and unitaries
v_{\Lambda\varepsilon},\tilde{v}_{\Lambda\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}

such that
\alpha=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\right)\circ\Xi_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\circ\left(\beta_{\Lambda}\otimes\beta_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)
,

\alpha^{-1}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\right)\circ\tilde{\Xi}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\circ\left(\tilde{\beta}_{\Lambda}\otimes\tilde{\beta}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)
.

For each
\eta\in{\mathcal{O}}_{i}
and
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, we fix some
\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\eta,\Lambda}
. We may and we do choose
\bar{V}_{\eta,\Lambda_{0}^{(i)}}=\mathbb{I}
,
\eta\in{\mathcal{O}}_{i}

and
\bar{V}_{\pi_{i},\Lambda}=\mathbb{I}
for
i=1,2
and any cone
\Lambda
.
For each objects
\rho_{1}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
,
\rho_{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{2}
and a cone
\Lambda
,
we fix
0<2\varepsilon<|\arg\Lambda|
and
set

\displaystyle Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda}:=\rho_{1}(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon})\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{-\varepsilon}}^{*}\pi_{1}\left(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}^{*}\right),\quad\tilde{Y}_{\rho_{2}}^{\Lambda}:=\rho_{2}(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon})\bar{V}_{\rho_{2},\Lambda_{-\varepsilon}}^{*}\pi_{2}\left(\tilde{v}_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}^{*}\right).

(6.1)

Due to the choice of
\bar{V}_{\pi_{i},\Lambda}=\mathbb{I}
, we have

Y_{\pi_{1}}^{\Lambda}=\mathbb{I}=\tilde{Y}_{\pi_{2}}^{\Lambda}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda}\right)^{*}\right)\circ\rho_{1}\circ\alpha|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}=\pi_{2}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}

(6.2)

because
for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho_{1}\circ\alpha(A)=\rho_{1}\circ\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\right)\circ\Xi_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\circ\left(\beta_{\Lambda}\otimes\beta_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)(A)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\rho_{1}\left(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\right)\right)\rho_{1}\left(\Xi_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\circ\left(\beta_{\Lambda}\otimes\beta_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)(A)\right)\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\rho_{1}\left(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\right)\bar{V}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{-\varepsilon}}^{*}\right)\pi_{1}\left(\Xi_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\circ\left(\beta_{\Lambda}\otimes\beta_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)(A)\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\rho_{1}(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon})\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{-\varepsilon}}^{*}\pi_{1}\left(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}^{*}\right)\right)\left(\pi_{2}(A)\right).\end{split}

(6.3)

Here we used
\Xi_{\Lambda,\varepsilon}\circ\left(\beta_{\Lambda}\otimes\beta_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)(A)\in\pi_{1}({\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\Lambda_{-\varepsilon}\right)^{c}})
.
Similarly, we get

\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\tilde{Y}_{\rho_{2}}^{\Lambda}\right)^{*}\right)\circ\rho_{2}\circ\alpha^{-1}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}=\pi_{1}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}}
.

From this, for each
\rho_{1}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
and
\rho_{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{2}

we get maps
F:{\mathcal{O}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{2}
,
G:{\mathcal{O}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
defined by

\displaystyle F(\rho_{1}):=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\circ\rho_{1}\circ\alpha\in{\mathcal{O}}_{2},\quad G(\rho_{2}):=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\tilde{Y}_{\rho_{2}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\right)\circ\rho_{2}\circ\alpha^{-1}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{1}.

(6.4)

Note that

\displaystyle GF(\rho_{1})=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\circ\rho_{1}.

(6.5)

We also have
F(\pi_{1})=\pi_{1}\alpha=\pi_{2}
and

G(\pi_{2})=\pi_{2}\alpha^{-1}=\pi_{1}
.
For morphisms
R_{1}\in(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})
with
\rho_{1},\sigma_{1}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
,
we have

\displaystyle F(R_{1}):=\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}R_{1}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\in\left(F(\rho_{1}),F(\sigma_{1})\right).

(6.6)

In fact for any
A\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}F(R_{1})\left(F(\rho_{1})(A)\right)=\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}R_{1}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\left(\rho_{1}\circ\alpha\left(A\right)\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}=\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\left(\sigma_{1}\circ\alpha\left(A\right)\right)R_{1}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\\
=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(\sigma_{1}\circ\alpha\left(A\right)\right)\cdot\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}R_{1}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}=\left(F(\sigma_{1})(A)\right)F(R_{1}).\end{split}

(6.7)

Similarly, for morphisms
R_{2}\in(\rho_{2},\sigma_{2})
with
\rho_{2},\sigma_{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{2}
,
we have

\displaystyle G(R_{2}):=\left(\tilde{Y}_{\sigma_{2}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}R_{2}\tilde{Y}_{\rho_{2}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\in\left(G(\rho_{2}),G(\sigma_{2})\right).

(6.8)

Note that
F,G
are linear on morphisms and

F(R_{1})^{*}=F(R_{1}^{*})
and
G(R_{2})^{*}=G(R_{2}^{*})
.
Hence we obtained a functor
F
from
C_{1}
to
C_{2}

and a functor
G
from
C_{2}
to
C_{1}
.
Any element of
(\pi_{i},\pi_{i})
is of the form
c\mathbb{I}
because
\pi_{i}

is irreducible
and

F(c\mathbb{I})=c\mathbb{I}
.

Next we would like to make
F,G
tensor functors.
To do so, first we note the domain
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}^{(i)}:=\overline{\cup_{\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}}\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime}}

of
T_{\rho_{i}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{0}^{(i)}\{\mathbb{I}\}}

for
i=1,2
are the same.
Recall from Lemma
2.4

that
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}^{(i)}=\overline{\cup_{\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}}\pi_{i}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}}
.
In fact
for any
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, choose
\varepsilon>0
with
\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, and
we have

\displaystyle\pi_{2}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)^{\prime\prime}=\left(\pi_{1}\circ\alpha\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)\right)^{\prime\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{1}(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon})\right)\left(\left(\pi_{1}\left(\Xi_{\Lambda\varepsilon}\beta_{\Lambda}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda}\right)\right)\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{1}(v_{\Lambda,\varepsilon})\right)\left(\pi_{1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}^{(1)}.

(6.9)

Hence we have
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}^{(2)}\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}^{(1)}
.
Similarly, we have
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}^{(1)}\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}^{(2)}
.
From now on we use the notation
{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}:={\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}^{(1)}={\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}^{(2}
.

Note that
Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}},\tilde{Y}_{\rho_{2}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
This is because of (
6.1
) and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho_{1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\right)\in T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\circ\pi_{1}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}})\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)},\quad\pi_{1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}}\right)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)},\\
&\bar{V}_{\rho_{1}\left(\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\right)_{-\varepsilon}}^{*}=\bar{V}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\bar{V}_{\rho_{1}\left(\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\right)_{-\varepsilon}}^{*}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}.\end{split}

(6.10)

Next we prove

\displaystyle T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}},\quad\text{for all}\quad\rho_{1}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{1}.

(6.11)

Set
X:=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}
.
By the definition of
F
, and
T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\circ\pi_{1}=\rho_{1}
,
we have
X\circ\pi_{2}=X\circ\pi_{1}\circ\alpha=F(\rho_{1})
.
For any cone
\Lambda\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, choose
\varepsilon>0

with
\Lambda_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
and
we have

\pi_{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\pi_{1}\left(v_{\Lambda\varepsilon}\right)\right)\left(\pi_{1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)
from (

Because

is

,
it is
\sigma
w-continuous on
\pi_{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda})^{\prime\prime}
.
From Lemma

,
we have
T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}=X
, proving (
6.11
).
Similarly, we have

\displaystyle T_{G(\rho_{2})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(Y_{\rho_{2}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\right)T_{\rho_{2}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}},\quad\text{for all}\quad\rho_{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{2}.

(6.12)

From
Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}},\tilde{Y}_{\rho_{2}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
, we can define unitaries

\displaystyle F_{2}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1}):=\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}},\quad\rho_{1},\sigma_{1}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{1}.

(6.13)

We then find using (
6.11
) that
for any
\rho_{1},\sigma_{1}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F(\rho_{1})\otimes F(\sigma_{1})=T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\circ T_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\circ\pi_{2}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\circ T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\circ T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\circ\pi_{2}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\circ T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\pi_{1}\circ\alpha\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(F_{2}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})^{*}\right)\circ F\left(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\right).\end{split}

(6.14)

Similarly, for
\rho_{2},\sigma_{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{2}
,
we have

\displaystyle G(\rho_{2})\otimes G(\sigma_{2})=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(G_{2}(\rho_{2},\sigma_{2})^{*}\right)\left(G\left(\rho_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}\right)\right)

(6.15)

with

\displaystyle G_{2}(\rho_{2},\sigma_{2}):=\left(\tilde{Y}_{\rho_{2}\otimes\sigma_{2}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{2}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\tilde{Y}_{\sigma_{2}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)\tilde{Y}_{\rho_{2}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}},\quad\rho_{2},\sigma_{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{2}.

(6.16)

We have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{2}\left(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1},\tau_{1}\right)F_{2}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})\\
&=\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\\
&\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\\
&=\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}},\end{split}

(6.17)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{2}\left(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}\right)T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\left(F_{2}(\sigma_{1},\tau_{1})\right)\\
&=\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)\\
&=\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\\
&=\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\\
&=\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\tau_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}.\end{split}

(6.18)

We used (
6.11
).
In the last line we used Lemma
3.6
.
Note that the right hand side of (
6.17
) and (
6.18
)
are the same.

Hence the following holds:

\begin{CD}\left(F(\rho_{1})\otimes F(\sigma_{1})\right)\otimes F(\tau_{1})@>{F_{2}\otimes\iota}>{}>F(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})\otimes F(\tau_{1})@>{F_{2}}>{}>F\left(\left(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\right)\otimes\tau_{1}\right)\\
@V{\mathbb{I}=a^{\prime}}V{}V@V{}V{F(a)=\mathbb{I}}V\\
F(\rho_{1})\otimes\left(F(\sigma_{1})\otimes F(\tau_{1})\right)@>{{\iota\otimes F_{2}}}>{}>F(\rho_{1})\otimes F(\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1})@>{{F_{2}}}>{}>F\left(\rho_{1}\otimes\left(\sigma_{1}\otimes\tau_{1}\right)\right)\end{CD},

where
a^{\prime}
and
F(a)
are associators which are
\mathbb{I}
.

Note from Lemma
2.14
(a)
that
T_{\pi_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits
.
For any
\rho_{1}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
, we then have

\displaystyle F_{2}\left(\pi_{1},\rho_{1}\right)=\left(Y_{\pi_{1}\otimes\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\pi_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\pi_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}=\mathbb{I}.

(6.19)

Hence the following holds:

\begin{CD}F(\pi_{1})\otimes F(\rho_{1})@>{F_{2}}>{}>F(\pi_{1}\otimes\rho_{1})\\
@A{{\mathbb{I}=F_{0}\otimes\iota}}A{}A@V{}V{F(\lambda_{1})=\mathbb{I}}V\\
\pi_{2}\otimes F(\rho_{1})@>{\lambda_{2}=\mathbb{I}}>{}>F(\rho_{1})\end{CD}

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle F_{2}(\rho_{1},\pi_{1})=\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\pi_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}(Y_{\pi_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}})\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}=\mathbb{I}.

(6.20)

Hence
F
is a unitary tensor functor.
Similarly,
G
is a unitary tensor functor.

From (
6.11
),(
6.12
),, we have

\displaystyle\eta(\rho_{1}):=\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\in\left(\rho_{1},GF(\rho_{1})\right)\cap\left(T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}},T_{GF(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\right).

(6.21)

Note that
\eta(\pi_{1})=\mathbb{I}
.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&GF(\rho_{1})\otimes GF(\sigma_{1})=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(G_{2}\left(F(\rho_{1}),F(\sigma_{1})\right)^{*}\right)\circ G\left(F(\rho_{1})\otimes F(\sigma_{1})\right)\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(G_{2}\left(F(\rho_{1}),F(\sigma_{1})\right)^{*}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})\otimes F(\sigma_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(F_{2}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})^{*}\right)F(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})\circ\alpha^{-1}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\begin{gathered}\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})\otimes F(\sigma_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)\\
\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})\otimes F(\sigma_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)\end{gathered}\right)\\
&\quad F(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})\circ\alpha^{-1}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\begin{gathered}\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)\end{gathered}\right)\\
&\quad F(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})\circ\alpha^{-1}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\begin{gathered}\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\right)T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)\\
\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\end{gathered}\right)\\
&\quad(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\begin{gathered}\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\end{gathered}\right)\\
&\quad(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\begin{gathered}\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(\tilde{Y}_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\right)^{*}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\right)\\
Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\tilde{Y}_{F(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\end{gathered}\right)GF(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(\left(\eta(\rho_{1})\otimes\eta(\sigma_{1})\right)\eta(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})^{*}\right)GF(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}).\end{split}

(6.22)

We used (
6.15
), (
6.4
), (
6.14
), (
6.16
), (
6.13
), (
6.5
)
occasionally.
In the fifth equation we used (
6.11
).
Setting

\displaystyle(GF)_{2}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1}):=\eta(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1})\left(\eta(\rho_{1})\otimes\eta(\sigma_{1})\right)^{*},

(6.23)

we conclude

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left((GF)_{2}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})\right)\circ\left(GF(\rho_{1})\otimes GF(\sigma_{1})\right)=GF(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}).

(6.24)

Because

\displaystyle(GF)_{2}\left(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1}\right)\cdot\left(\eta(\rho_{1})\otimes\eta(\sigma_{1})\right)=\eta(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}),

(6.25)

we see that

\begin{CD}\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}@>{id}>{}>\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\\
@V{\eta\otimes\eta}V{}V@V{}V{\eta}V\\
GF(\rho_{1})\otimes GF(\sigma_{1})@>{{(GF)_{2}}}>{}>GF\left(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\right).\end{CD}

holds.
Hence we see
GF\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits
.
Similarly we have
FG\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits
.
Hence
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are unitarily monoidally equivalent.

Finally, we show that
F
is braided.
Let
\rho_{1},\sigma_{1}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
.
We would like to show

\displaystyle\begin{CD}F(\rho_{1})\otimes F(\sigma_{1})@>{F_{2}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})}>{}>F\left(\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}\right)\\
@V{{\epsilon_{+}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(F(\rho_{1}),F(\sigma_{1})\right)}}V{}V@V{}V{F\left(\epsilon_{+}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})\right)}V\\
F(\sigma_{1})\otimes F(\rho_{1})@>{{F_{2}(\sigma_{1},\rho_{1})}}>{}>F\left(\sigma_{1}\otimes\rho_{1}\right).\end{CD}

(6.26)

Fix

\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
,
satisfying
\Lambda_{1}\perp_{(\theta,\varphi)}\Lambda_{2}
,

\Lambda_{2}\leftarrow_{(\theta,\varphi)}\Lambda_{1}
.
For each
t_{j}\geq 0
,
j=1,2
, we fix
\bar{V}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{j}+{t_{j}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{j}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{j}+{t_{j}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{j}}}
,
\bar{V}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{j}+{t_{j}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{j}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{j}+{t_{j}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{j}}}

and

\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1})\Lambda_{j}+{t_{j}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{j}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{F(\rho_{1})\Lambda_{j}+{t_{j}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{j}}}
,
\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1})\Lambda_{j}+{t_{j}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{j}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{F(\sigma_{1})\Lambda_{j}+{t_{j}}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{j}}}
.
Set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&W_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}:=\bar{V}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}\bar{V}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}^{*}=\bar{V}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}},\quad W_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}:=\bar{V}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}}\bar{V}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}^{*}=\bar{V}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}},\\
&W_{F(\rho_{1})\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}:=\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1})\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1})\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}^{*}=\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1})\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}},\quad W_{F(\sigma_{1})\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}:=\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1})\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}}\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1})\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}^{*}=\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1})\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}}.\end{split}

(6.27)

With these notation, (
6.26
) means

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}\otimes\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\\
&\lim_{\bm{t}\to\infty}T_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(W_{F(\rho_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(W_{F(\sigma_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}W_{F(\rho_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\left(W_{F(\sigma_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)\\
&=\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}\otimes\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\lim_{\bm{t}\to\infty}\left(W_{F(\sigma_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\otimes W_{F(\rho_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\left(W_{F(\rho_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\otimes W_{F(\sigma_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)\\
&=F_{2}(\sigma_{1},\rho_{1})\epsilon_{+}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(F(\rho_{1}),F(\sigma_{1})\right)\\
&=F\left(\epsilon_{+}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})\right)F_{2}(\rho_{1},\sigma_{1})\\
&=\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}\otimes\rho_{1}}^{{\Lambda_{0}}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\lim_{\bm{t}\to\infty}\left(W_{\sigma_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\otimes W_{\rho_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\left(W_{\rho_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\otimes W_{\sigma_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\\
&=\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}\otimes\rho_{1}}^{{\Lambda_{0}}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}\lim_{\bm{t}\to\infty}T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(W_{\rho_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(W_{\sigma_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\left(W_{\rho_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(W_{\sigma_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)\right)\\
&Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}\otimes\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)^{*}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\end{split}

(6.28)

This can be rewritten to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\bm{t}\to\infty}\left\|\begin{gathered}\left(W_{\sigma_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(W_{\rho_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)T_{\sigma_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\\
T_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(W_{F(\rho_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(W_{F(\sigma_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\\
-\left(W_{\rho_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(W_{\sigma_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)\right)\\
T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\left(Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\left(\left(W_{F(\sigma_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\right)\left(W_{F(\rho_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\end{gathered}\right\|=0.\end{split}

(6.29)

Using (
6.11
) and
W_{\rho_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\in\left(T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}},T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\right)
etc,
the first term in the norm is equal to

\displaystyle\left(W_{\sigma_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(W_{F(\sigma_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}T_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}},\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1}),\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}}}\left(W_{\rho_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(W_{F(\rho_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\right).

(6.30)

The second term is equal to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&W_{\rho_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(W_{F(\rho_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1}),\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\left(W_{\sigma_{1}{\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(W_{F(\sigma_{1}){\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\right).\end{split}

(6.31)

Setting

\displaystyle{X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}:=W_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(W_{F(\rho_{1})\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*},\quad{X^{t_{2}}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}}:=W_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}Y_{\sigma_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(W_{F(\sigma_{1})\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\Lambda_{2}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*},

(6.32)

(
6.29
) becomes

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\left\|{X^{t_{2}}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}}T_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}},\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1}),\Lambda_{2}+t_{2}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}}}\left({X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}\right)-{X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}},\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1}),\Lambda_{1}+t_{1}\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}}\left({X^{t_{2}}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}}\right)\right\|=0.

(6.33)

This is the equation we have to show now.

In order to do so, fix some
\delta>0

such that
\arg(\Lambda_{1})_{64\delta}\cap\arg(\Lambda_{2})_{64\delta}=\emptyset

and
(\Lambda_{1})_{64\delta},(\Lambda_{2})_{64\delta}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
.
We set

\mathfrak{A}_{i}(\Lambda):=\pi_{i}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\Lambda^{c}}\right)^{\prime}
,

i=1,2
.

Fix any
\epsilon>0
.
Recall
g_{\varphi,\delta,\delta^{\prime}}
from Definition
1.2
.
Fix some
s\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&s\geq R_{|\arg\Lambda_{i}|+10\delta,\delta}\\
&g_{|\arg\Lambda_{1}|+2\delta,\delta,\delta}(s)<\frac{\epsilon}{32},\\
&g_{|\arg\Lambda_{i}|+12\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{s}{2}\right)+2g_{|\arg\Lambda_{i}|+16\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{s}{2}\right)+f_{|\arg\Lambda_{i}|+12\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{s}{2}\right)<\frac{\epsilon}{32},\\
&g_{|\arg\Lambda_{i}|+22\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{s}{2}\right)+2g_{|\arg\Lambda_{i}|+26\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{s}{2}\right)+f_{|\arg\Lambda_{i}|+22\delta,\delta,\delta}\left(\frac{s}{2}\right)<\frac{\epsilon}{32},\end{split}

(6.34)

for
i=1,2
.
Now for any
t_{1}\geq 0
, we claim that
there is some unitary

z^{t_{1}s}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}\in\mathfrak{A}_{2}\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}\right)_{9\delta}+(t_{1}-2s)\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\left\|z^{t_{1}s}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}-{X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}\right\|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}.

(6.35)

To prove this, set
\Lambda_{j}^{t}:=\Lambda_{j}+t\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{j}}

for each
t\geq 0
and
j=1,2
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left({X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}\right)\pi_{2}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\left(W_{F(\rho_{1})\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)^{*}\right)\pi_{2}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)F(\rho_{1})|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}Y_{\rho_{1}}^{\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}}\right)T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(2)}\mathbb{I}}\circ\pi_{2}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(W_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\Lambda_{1}}^{\bm{t}}\right)T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi)\Lambda_{0}^{(1)}\mathbb{I}}\circ\pi_{2}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}\\
&=T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\circ\pi_{2}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}=T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\circ\pi_{1}\alpha|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\pi_{1}\left(v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}}\delta}\right)\right)\circ T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\pi_{1}\left(\Xi_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}},\delta}\circ\beta_{({\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}})^{c}}\right)|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\pi_{1}\left(v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}}\delta}\right)\right)\pi_{1}\circ\left(\Xi_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}},\delta}\circ\beta_{({\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}})^{c}}\right)|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\pi_{1}\left(v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}}\delta}\right)\cdot\pi_{1}\left(v^{*}_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}}\delta}\right)\right)\pi_{1}\alpha|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}\\
&=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\pi_{1}\left(v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}}\delta}\right)\cdot\pi_{1}\left(v^{*}_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}}\delta}\right)\right)\pi_{2}|_{{\mathcal{A}}_{\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta}\right)^{c}}}.\end{split}

(6.36)

Hence we have

\displaystyle{X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}=T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\pi_{1}\left(v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}}\delta}\right)\cdot\pi_{1}\left(v^{*}_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}}\delta}\right)\cdot y^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}},

(6.37)

with some unitary
y^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}\in\mathfrak{A}_{2}(\left(\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}\right)_{\delta})
.
Recall from Definition
1.2
that
there is a unitary

\displaystyle v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}},\delta,\delta,s}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{3\delta}-s\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}

(6.38)

such that

\displaystyle\left\|v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}},\delta}-v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}},\delta,\delta,s}^{\prime}\right\|\leq g_{|\arg\Lambda_{1}|+2\delta,\delta,\delta}(s)<\frac{\epsilon}{32}.

(6.39)

Because
{\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\subset\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{3\delta}-s\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\pi_{1}\left(v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}},\delta,\delta,s}^{\prime}\right),T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\left(\pi_{1}\left(v_{{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{\delta}},\delta,\delta,s}^{\prime}\right)\right)\\
&\in T_{\rho_{1}}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{\rho_{1},\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\left(\pi_{1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{3\delta}-s\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}\right)\right)\subset\mathfrak{A}_{1}\left({\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{4\delta}-s\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}\right).\end{split}

(6.40)

from Lemma
2.16
.
Note from Lemma
1.4
and our choice of
s
(
6.34
)
there is some unitary
w_{s}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\|w_{s}-\mathbb{I}\right\|<\frac{\epsilon}{32},\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}\nolimits\left(w_{s}\right)\left(\mathfrak{A}_{1}\left({\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{4\delta}-s\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}\right)\right)\subset\mathfrak{A}_{2}\left({\left({\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}\right)_{9\delta}-2s\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}}\right).\end{split}

(6.41)

From (
6.37
), (
6.38
), (
6.39
), (
6.40
),(
6.41
),
we conclude the claim (
6.35
).
Similarly,
for any
t\geq 0
,
there is some unitary

z^{t_{2}s}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}\in\mathfrak{A}_{2}\left(\left(\Lambda_{2}\right)_{9\delta}+(t_{2}-2s)\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\left\|z^{t_{2}s}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}-{X^{t_{2}}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}}\right\|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}.

(6.42)

From this, we can estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|{X^{t_{2}}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}}T_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{2}^{t_{2}},\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1}),\Lambda_{2}^{t_{2}}}}\left({X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}\right)-{X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1}),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\left({X^{t_{2}}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}}\right)\right\|\\
&\leq{\epsilon}+\left\|z^{t_{2}s}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}T_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{2}^{t_{2}},\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1}),\Lambda_{2}^{t_{2}}}}\left(z^{t_{1}s}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}\right)-z^{t_{1}s}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1}),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\left(z^{t_{2}s}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}\right)\right\|\\
&\leq{\epsilon}+\left\|\left.T_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{2}^{t_{2}},\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1}),\Lambda_{2}^{t_{2}}}}\right|_{\mathfrak{A}_{2}\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}\right)_{9\delta}+(t_{1}-2s)\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{A}_{2}\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}\right)_{9\delta}+(t_{1}-2s)\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right)}\right\|\\
&+\left\|\left.T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1}),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\right|_{\mathfrak{A}_{2}\left(\left(\Lambda_{2}\right)_{9\delta}+(t_{2}-2s)\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)}-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\mathfrak{A}_{2}\left(\left(\Lambda_{2}\right)_{9\delta}+(t_{2}-2s)\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right)}\right\|\\
&+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathfrak{A}_{2}\left(\left(\Lambda_{2}\right)_{9\delta}+(t_{2}-2s)\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{2}}\right),\\
y\in\mathfrak{A}_{2}\left(\left(\Lambda_{1}\right)_{9\delta}+(t_{1}-2s)\bm{e}_{\Lambda_{1}}\right),\\
\left\|x\right\|,\left\|y\right\|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\|xy-yx\right\|.\end{split}

(6.43)

From Lemma
2.19
, Lemma
2.20
,
the last three terms in the last line of (
6.43
) converges to
0

as
t_{1},t_{2}\to\infty
.
Therefore, for
t_{1},t_{2}
large enough,
we have

\displaystyle\left\|{X^{t_{2}}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}}T_{F(\sigma_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{2}^{t_{2}},\bar{V}_{F(\sigma_{1}),\Lambda_{2}^{t_{2}}}}\left({X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}\right)-{X^{t_{1}}_{\rho_{1}\Lambda_{1}}}T_{F(\rho_{1})}^{(\theta,\varphi),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}},\bar{V}_{F(\rho_{1}),\Lambda_{1}^{t_{1}}}}\left({X^{t_{2}}_{\sigma_{1}\Lambda_{2}}}\right)\right\|\leq\epsilon.

(6.44)

This proves
(
6.33
) hence (
6.26
).
Hence our monoidal equivalence
F
is braided.

\square
