Appendix A
Cones

We cpllect basic notation and properties of cones.
For
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
, we set

{\mathbb{A}}_{I}:=\left\{e^{it}\mid t\in I\right\}\subset{\mathbb{T}}.

We say
{\mathbb{A}}\subset{\mathbb{T}}
is a closed interval if it is
{\mathbb{A}}={\mathbb{A}}_{I}

with a closed interval
I
of
{\mathbb{R}}
.
For
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we set
\bm{e}_{\theta}=(\cos\theta,\sin\theta)
.
For
\bm{a}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
,
set

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\bm{a},\theta,\varphi}:=

\displaystyle\left\{\bm{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mid(\bm{x}-\bm{a})\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}>\cos\varphi\cdot\left\|\bm{x}-{\bm{a}}\right\|\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{a}+\left\{t\bm{e}_{\beta}\mid t>0,\quad\beta\in(\theta-\varphi,\theta+\varphi)\right\}.

We call a subset of
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
with this form a
cone
.
For a cone
\Lambda=\Lambda_{\bm{a},\theta,\varphi}
given above,
we set

\displaystyle\arg\left(\Lambda\right):=\left\{e^{it}\in{\mathbb{T}}\mid t\in[\theta-\varphi,\theta+\varphi]\right\},\quad|\arg\left(\Lambda\right)|:=2\varphi,\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{a}_{\Lambda}:=\bm{a},\quad\bm{e}_{\Lambda}:=\bm{e}_{\theta}.

For
\varepsilon>0
and
\Lambda=\Lambda_{\bm{a},\theta,\varphi}
with
\varphi+\varepsilon<\pi

we denote the “fattened” cone by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\varepsilon}:=\Lambda_{\bm{a},\theta,\varphi+\varepsilon}.

If
0<\varphi-\varepsilon
, we also set

\displaystyle\Lambda_{-\varepsilon}:=\Lambda_{\bm{a},\theta,\varphi-\varepsilon}.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\kappa\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
\bm{a}_{1},\bm{a}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and

\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in(0,\pi)

with

\displaystyle\kappa\leq\theta_{1}-\varphi_{1},\theta_{2}-\varphi_{2},\quad\text{and}\quad\theta_{1}+\varphi_{1},\theta_{2}+\varphi_{2}<\kappa+2\pi.

Set

\displaystyle\eta:=\max\left\{\theta_{1}+\varphi_{1},\theta_{2}+\varphi_{2}\right\},

\displaystyle\zeta:=\min\left\{\theta_{1}-\varphi_{1},\theta_{2}-\varphi_{2}\right\},

and

\displaystyle\theta:=\frac{\eta+\zeta}{2},\quad\varphi:=\frac{\eta-\zeta}{2}.

Assume that
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
.
Then there is some
R_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}

such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\bm{a}_{1},\theta_{1},\varphi_{1}},\Lambda_{\bm{a}_{2},\theta_{2},\varphi_{2}}\subset\Lambda_{-R\bm{e}_{\theta},\theta,\varphi},

for all
R\geq R_{0}
.

By the definition of
\theta,\varphi
, we have

\displaystyle(\theta_{i}-\varphi_{i},\theta_{i}+\varphi_{i})\subset(\theta-\varphi,\theta+\varphi),\quad i=1,2.

(A.1)

Any element of
\Lambda_{\bm{a}_{i},\theta_{i},\varphi_{i}}
,
i=1,2

is of the form

\displaystyle\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta},\quad\beta\in(\theta_{i}-\varphi_{i},\theta_{i}+\varphi_{i}),\quad 0\leq t\in{\mathbb{R}}.

We have to show that there is some
R_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left(\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}-(-R\bm{e}_{\theta})\right)\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}>\cos\varphi\cdot\left\|\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}-(-R\bm{e}_{\theta})\right\|

for any
\beta\in(\theta_{i}-\varphi_{i},\theta_{i}+\varphi_{i})
,

0\leq t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
i=1,2
and
R\geq R_{0}
.

Suppose that
\varphi\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2}]
.
Note that
-\cos\varphi+\cos(\beta-\theta)\geq 0

because of (
A.1
).
Using this (in the third line), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left(\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}-(-R\bm{e}_{\theta})\right)\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}-\cos\varphi\cdot\left\|\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}-(-R\bm{e}_{\theta})\right\|\\
&\geq\bm{a}_{i}\cdot e_{\theta}+t\cos(\beta-\theta)+R-\cos\varphi\left(\left\|\bm{a}_{i}\right\|+t+R\right)\\
&\geq\bm{a}_{i}\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}-\cos\varphi\left\|\bm{a}_{i}\right\|+t\left(-\cos\varphi+\cos(\beta-\theta)\right)+R(1-\cos\varphi)\\
&\geq\bm{a}_{i}\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}-\cos\varphi\left\|\bm{a}_{i}\right\|+R(1-\cos\varphi)\end{split}

The last term is positive if

\displaystyle R>(1-\cos\varphi)^{-1}\max_{i=1,2}\left\{-\bm{a}_{i}\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}+\cos\varphi\left\|\bm{a}_{i}\right\|\right\}.

If
\varphi\in(\frac{\pi}{2},\pi)
, note that there is some
R_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
such that

\displaystyle\left(-R\bm{e}_{\theta}-\bm{a}_{i}\right)\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}<\cos\varphi\left\|R\bm{e}_{\theta}+\bm{a}_{i}\right\|,

(A.2)

for all
R\geq R_{0}
and
i=1,2
.
If
R\geq R_{0}
then
we have

\displaystyle\left(\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}-(-R\bm{e}_{\theta})\right)\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}=\left(R\bm{e}_{\theta}+\bm{a}_{i}\right)\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}>-\cos\varphi\left\|R\bm{e}_{\theta}+\bm{a}_{i}\right\|+t\cos(\beta-\theta).

(A.3)

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}-(-R\bm{e}_{\theta})\right\|\geq\left\|t\bm{e}_{\beta}\right\|-\left\|R\bm{e}_{\theta}+\bm{a}_{i}\right\|=t-\left\|R\bm{e}_{\theta}+\bm{a}_{i}\right\|.

(A.4)

From
\cos\varphi<0
, we then have

\displaystyle\cos\varphi\left(t-\left\|R\bm{e}_{\theta}+\bm{a}_{i}\right\|\right)\geq\cos\varphi\cdot\left\|\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}-(-R\bm{e}_{\theta})\right\|.

(A.5)

Substituting this to (
A.3
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}-(-R\bm{e}_{\theta})\right)\cdot\bm{e}_{\theta}>-\cos\varphi\left\|R\bm{e}_{\theta}+\bm{a}_{i}\right\|+t\cos(\beta-\theta)\\
\geq\cos\varphi\left(-\left\|R\bm{e}_{\theta}+\bm{a}_{i}\right\|+t\right)\geq\cos\varphi\cdot\left\|\bm{a}_{i}+t\bm{e}_{\beta}-(-R\bm{e}_{\theta})\right\|,\end{split}

(A.6)

proving the claim.

\square

Let us give another version of the Lemma.

Lemma A.2
.

For any
\bm{a},\bm{a}_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,
\theta,\theta_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and

\varphi,\varphi_{1}\in(0,\pi)

with

\displaystyle\theta-\varphi\leq\theta_{1}-\varphi_{1}<\theta_{1}+\varphi_{1}\leq\theta+\varphi.

Then there is some
R_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}

such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{R\bm{e}_{\theta_{1}}+\bm{a}_{1},\theta_{1},\varphi_{1}}\subset\Lambda_{\bm{a},\theta,\varphi},

for all
R\geq R_{0}
.

Set
\tilde{\theta}:=-\theta_{1}
,
\tilde{\varphi}:=\pi-\varphi_{1}
,

\tilde{\theta}_{1}:=-\theta
,
\tilde{\varphi}_{1}:=\pi-\varphi
.
We have

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}-\tilde{\varphi}\leq\tilde{\theta}_{1}-\tilde{\varphi}_{1}<\tilde{\theta}_{1}+\tilde{\varphi}_{1}\leq\tilde{\theta}+\tilde{\varphi}.

Then applying Lemma
A.1
, (with
\theta_{1},\varphi_{1},\theta_{2},\varphi_{2}
,
\bm{a}_{1}

replaced by

\tilde{\theta}_{1}
,
\tilde{\varphi}_{1}
,
\tilde{\theta}
,
\tilde{\varphi}
,
-\bm{a}_{1}+\bm{a}
respectively),
there is some
R_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{-\bm{a}_{1}+\bm{a},-\theta,\pi-\varphi}=\Lambda_{-\bm{a}_{1}+\bm{a},\tilde{\theta}_{1},\tilde{\varphi}_{1}},\subset\Lambda_{-R\bm{e}_{\tilde{\theta}},\tilde{\theta},\tilde{\varphi}}=\Lambda_{R\bm{e}_{\theta_{1}},-\theta_{1},\pi-\varphi_{1}},

for all
R\geq R_{0}
.
Taking its complement, we obtain

\displaystyle\Lambda_{R\bm{e}_{\theta_{1}},\theta_{1},\varphi_{1}}\subset\Lambda_{-\bm{a}_{1}+\bm{a},\theta,\varphi},

for all
R\geq R_{0}
, proving the claim.

\square

Let
\theta,\varphi\in{\mathbb{R}}
with
0<\varphi<\pi
.
We define a set of cones
{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}:=\left\{\Lambda\mid\arg\Lambda\cap{\mathbb{A}}_{[\theta-\varphi,\theta+\varphi]}=\emptyset\right\}.

(A.7)

Lemma A.3
.

Let
\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\varphi\in(0,\pi)
.
Then
{\mathcal{C}}_{(\theta,\varphi)}
is a upward filtering set
with respect to the inclusion order.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle\cup_{R\geq 0}\left(\Lambda-R\bm{e}_{\Lambda}\right)={\mathbb{R}}^{2}.

(A.8)

Immediate from Lemma
A.1
.

\square
